<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 2—MATERIALS PROCESSING</th></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>Technical Notes to 2A001 to 2A006:<br>1. DN is the product of the bearing bore diameter in mm and the bearing rotational velocity in rpm.<br>2. Operating temperatures include those temperatures obtained when a gas turbine engine has stopped after operation.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A001</strong></td><td>Ball bearings or solid roller bearings (except tapered roller bearings) having tolerances specified by the manufacturer in accordance with Annular Bearing Engineers Committee (ABEC) 7, ABEC 7P, ABEC 7T or ISO Standard Class 4 or better (or equivalents), and having any of the following characteristics:<br>a. Rings, balls or rollers made from monel or beryllium;<br>b. Manufactured for use at operating temperatures above 573 K (300°C) either by using special materials or by special heat treatment; or<br>c. With lubricating elements or component modifications that, according to the manufacturer’s specifications, are specially designed to enable the bearings to operate at speeds exceeding 2.3 million DN.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A002</strong></td><td>Other ball bearings or solid roller bearings (except tapered roller bearings) having tolerances specified by the manufacturer in accordance with Annular Bearing Engineers Committee (ABEC) 9, ABEC 9P or ISO Standard Class 2 or better (or equivalents).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A003</strong></td><td>Solid tapered roller bearings, having tolerances specified by the manufacturer in accordance with American National Standards Institute (ANSI) / Anti-Friction Bearing Manufacturers Association (AFBMA) Class 00 (inch) or Class A (metric) or better (or equivalents) and having either of the following characteristics:<br>a. With lubricating elements or component modifications that, according to the manufacturer’s specifications, are specially designed to enable the bearings to operate at speeds exceeding 2.3 million DN; or<br>b. Manufactured for use at operating temperatures below 219 K (−54°C) or above 423 K (150°C).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A004</strong></td><td>Gas-lubricated foil bearings manufactured for use at operating temperatures of 561 K (288°C) or higher and with a unit load capacity exceeding 1 MPa.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A005</strong></td><td>Active magnetic bearing systems.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A006</strong></td><td>Fabric-lined self-aligning or fabric-lined journal sliding bearings manufactured for use at operating temperatures below 219 K (−54°C) or above 423 K (150°C).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A225</strong></td><td>Crucibles made of materials resistant to liquid actinide metals, as follows:<br>a. Crucibles with a volume of between 150 ml and 8 litres and made of or coated with any of the following materials having a purity of 98% or greater:<br>1. Calcium fluoride (CaF<sub>2</sub>);<br>2. Calcium zirconate (metazirconate) (Ca<sub>2</sub>ZrO<sub>3</sub>);<br>3. Cerium sulphide (Ce<sub>2</sub>S<sub>3</sub>);<br>4. Erbium oxide (erbia) (Er<sub>2</sub>O<sub>3</sub>);<br>5. Hafnium oxide (hafnia) (HfO<sub>2</sub>);<br>6. Magnesium oxide (MgO);<br>7. Nitrided niobium-titanium-tungsten alloy (approximately 50% Nb, 30%Ti, 20%W);<br>8. Yttrium oxide (yttria) (Y<sub>2</sub>O<sub>3</sub>); or<br>9. Zirconium oxide (zirconia) (ZrO<sub>2</sub>);<br>b. Crucibles with a volume of between 50 ml and 2 litres and made of or lined with tantalum, having a purity of 99.9% or greater;<br>c. Crucibles with a volume of between 50 ml and 2 litres and made of or lined with tantalum (having a purity of 98% or greater) coated with tantalum carbide, nitride or boride (or any combination of these).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2A226</strong></td><td>Valves 5 mm or greater in diameter, with a bellows seal, wholly made of or lined with aluminum, aluminum alloy, nickel or alloy containing 60% or more nickel, either manually or automatically operated.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>NOTE: 2B001 to 2B009 do not control measuring interferometer systems, without closed or open loop feedback, containing a “laser” to measure slide movement errors of machine-tools, dimensional inspection machines or similar equipment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B001</strong></td><td>“Numerical control” units, “motion control boards” specially designed for “numerical control” applications on machine tools, machine tools, and specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>Technical Notes:<br>1. Secondary parallel contouring axes, e.g., the w-axis on horizontal boring mills or a secondary rotary axis the centre line of which is parallel to the primary rotary axis, are not counted in the total number of contouring axes.<br>N.B.: Rotary axes need not rotate over 360°. A rotary axis can be driven by a linear device, e.g., a screw or a rack-and-pinion.<br>2. Axis nomenclature shall be in accordance with International Standard ISO 841, ‘Numerical Control Machines—Axis and Motion Nomenclature’.<br>a. “Numerical control” units for machine tools, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:<br>NOTE: 2B001.a. does not control “numerical control” units:<br>a. Modified for and incorporated in machines not specified in this item; or<br>b. Specially designed for machines not specified in this item.<br>1. Having more than four interpolating axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”;<br>2. Having two, three or four interpolating axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control” and:<br>a. Capable of “real time processing” of data to modify, during the machining operation, tool path, feed rate and spindle data by either:<br>1. Automatic calculation and modification of part programme data for machining in two or more axes by means of measuring cycles and access to source data; or<br>2. “Adaptive control” with more than one physical variable measured and processing by means of a computing model (strategy) to change one or more machining instructions to optimize the process;<br>b. Capable of receiving directly (on-line) and processing computer aided design (CAD) data for internal preparation of machine instructions; or<br>c. Capable, without modification, according to the manufacturer’s technical specifications, of accepting additional boards which would permit an increase above the control levels specified in 2B001, in the number of interpolating axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”, even if they do not contain these additional boards;<br>3. “Motion control boards” specially designed for machine tools and having any of the following characteristics:<br>1. Interpolation in more than four axes;<br>2. Capable of “real time processing” as described in 2B001.a.2.a.; or<br>3. Capable of receiving and processing CAD data as described in 2B001.a.2.b.;<br>4. Machine tools, as follows, for removing or cutting metals, ceramics or composites, which, according to the manufacturer’s technical specifications, can be equipped with electronic devices for simultaneous “contouring control” in two or more axes:<br>1. Machine tools for turning, grinding, milling or any combination thereof which:<br>a. Have two or more axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”; and<br>b. Have any of the following characteristics:<br>1. Two or more contouring rotary axes;<br>Technical Note: The c axis on jig grinders used to maintain grinding wheels normal to the work surface is not considered a contouring rotary axis.<br>2. One or more contouring “tilting spindles”;<br>NOTE: 2B001.c.1.b.2. applies to machine tools for grinding or milling only.<br>3. “Camming” (axial displacement) in one revolution of the spindle less (better) than 0.0006 mm total indicator reading (TIR);<br>NOTE: 2B001.c.1.b.3. applies to machine tools for turning only.<br>4. “Run out” (out-of-true running) in one revolution of the spindle less (better) than 0.0006 mm TIR;<br>5. The positioning accuracies, with all compensations available, are less (better) than:<br>a. 0.001° on any rotary axis; or<br>b. 1. 0.004 mm along any linear axis (overall positioning) for grinding machines;<br>2. 0.006 mm along any linear axis (overall positioning) for turning or milling machines; or<br>NOTE: 2B001.c.1.b.5. does not control milling or turning machine tools with a positioning accuracy along one axis, with all compensations available, equal to or more (worse) than 0.005 mm.<br>Technical Note:<br>The positioning accuracy of “numerically controlled” machine tools is to be determined and presented in accordance with ISO/DIS 230/2, paragraph 2.13, in conjunction with the requirements below:<br>a. Test conditions (paragraph 3):<br>1. For 12 hours before and during measurements, the machine tool and accuracy measuring equipment will be kept at the same ambient temperature. During the premeasurement time the slides of the machine will be continuously cycled in the same manner that the accuracy measurements will be taken;<br>2. The machine shall be equipped with any mechanical, electronic, or software compensation to be exported with the machine;<br>3. Accuracy of measuring equipment for the measurements shall be at least four times more accurate than the expected machine tool accuracy;<br>4. Power supply for slide drives shall be as follows:<br>a. Line voltage variation shall not exceed ±10% of nominal rated voltage;<br>b. Frequency variation shall not exceed ±2Hz of normal frequency;<br>c. Lineouts or interrupted service are not permitted;<br>b. Test programme (paragraph 4):<br>1. Feed rate (velocity of slides) during measurement shall be the rapid traverse rate;<br>N.B.: In the case of machine tools which generate optical quality surfaces, the feed rate shall be equal to or less than 50 mm per minute.<br>2. Measurements shall be made in an incremental manner from one limit of the axis travel to the other without returning to the starting position for each move to the target position;<br>3. Axes not being measured shall be retained at mid travel during test of an axis;<br>c. Presentation of test results (paragraph 2):<br>The results of the measurements must include:<br>1. positioning accuracy (A); and<br>2. The mean reversal error (B).<br>6. a. A positioning accuracy less (better) than 0.007 mm; and<br>b. A slide motion from rest for all slides within 20% of a motion command input for inputs of less than 0.5 micrometre.<br>Technical Note:<br>Minimum increment of motion test (slide motion from rest):<br>The test is conducted only if the machine tool is equipped with a control unit the minimum increment of which is less (better) than 0.5 micrometre. Prepare the machine for testing in accordance with ISO 230/2 paragraphs 3.1, 3.2, 3.3.<br>Conduct the test on each axis (slide) of the machine tool as follows:<br>a. Move the axis over at least 50% of the maximum travel in plus and minus directions twice at maximum feed rate, rapid traverse rate or jog control;<br>b. Wait at least 10 seconds;<br>c. With manual data input, input the minimum programmable increment of the control unit;<br>d. Measure the axis movement;<br>e. Clear the control unit with the servo null, reset or whatever clears any signal (voltage) in the servo loop;<br>f. Repeat steps b. to e. five times, twice in the same direction of the axis travel and three times in the opposite direction of travel for a total of six test points;<br>g. If the axis movement is between 80% and 120% of the minimum programmable input for four of the six test points, the machine is controlled.<br>For rotary axes, the measurement is taken 200 mm from the centre of rotation.<br>Notes:<br>1.. 2B001.c.1. does not control cylindrical external, internal and external-internal grinding machines having all of the following characteristics:<br>a. Not centreless (shoe-type) grinding machines;<br>b. Limited to cylindrical grinding;<br>c. A maximum workpiece capacity of 150 mm outside diameter or length;<br>d. Only two axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”; and<br>e. No contouring c axis.<br>2.. 2B001.c.1. does not control machines designed specifically as jig grinders having both of the following characteristics:<br>a. Axes limited to x, y, c and a, where the c axis is used to maintain the grinding wheel normal to the work surface and the a axis is configured to grind barrel cams; and<br>b. A spindle “run out” not less (not better) than 0.0006 mm.<br>3.. 2B001.c.1. does not control tool or cutter grinding machines having all of the following characteristics:<br>a. Shipped as a complete system with “software” specially designed for the production of tools or cutters;<br>b. No more than two rotary axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”;<br>c. “Run out” (out-of-true running) in one revolution of the spindle not less (not better) than 0.0006 mm TIR; and<br>d. The positioning accuracies, with all compensations available, are not less (not better) than:<br>1. 0.004 mm along any linear axis for overall positioning; or<br>2. 0.001° on any rotary axis.<br>2. Electrical discharge machines (EDM) of the wire feed type which have five or more axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”;<br>3. Electrical discharge machines (EDM) of the non-wire type which have two or more rotary axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”;<br>4. Machine tools for removing metals, ceramics or composites:<br>a. By means of:<br>1. Water or other liquid jets, including those employing abrasive additives;<br>2. Electron beam; or<br>3. “Laser” beam; and<br>b. Having two or more rotary axes which:<br>1. Can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”; and<br>2. Have a positioning accuracy of less (better) than 0.003°.<br>Technical Note: Machines capable of being simultaneously coordinated for contouring control in two or more rotary axes or one or more “tilting spindles”, are specified in this item regardless of the number of simultaneously coordinated contouring axes that can be controlled by the “numerical control” units attached to the machine.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B002</strong></td><td>Non-“numerically controlled” machine tools for generating optical quality surfaces, as follows:<br>a. Turning machines using a single point cutting tool and having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. Slide positioning accuracy less (better) than 0.0005 mm per 300 mm of travel;<br>2. Bidirectional slide positioning repeatability less (better) than 0.00025 mm per 300 mm of travel;<br>3. Spindle “run out” and “camming” less (better) than 0.0004 mm TIR;<br>4. Angular deviation of the slide movement (yaw, pitch and roll) less (better) than 2 seconds of arc, TIR, over full travel; and<br>5. Slide perpendicularity less (better) than 0.001 mm per 300 mm of travel;<br>Technical Note: The bidirectional slide positioning repeatability (R) of an axis is the maximum value of the repeatability of positioning at any position along or around the axis determined using the procedure and under the conditions specified in part 2.11 of ISO 230/2: 1988.<br>b. Fly cutting machines having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Spindle “run out” and “camming” less (better) than 0.0004 mm TIR; and<br>2. Angular deviation of slide movement (yaw, pitch and roll) less (better) than 2 seconds of arc, TIR, over full travel.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B003</strong></td><td>“Numerically controlled” or manual machine tools specially designed for cutting, finishing, grinding or honing either of the following classes of bevel or parallel axis hardened (R<sub>c</sub>=40 or more) gears, and specially designed components, controls and accessories therefor:<br>a. Hardened bevel gears finished to a quality of better than American Gear Manufacturers Association (AGMA) 13 (equivalent to ISO 1328 class 4); or<br>b. Hardened spur, helical and double-helical gears with a pitch diameter exceeding 1,250 mm and a face width of 15% of pitch diameter or larger finished to a quality of AGMA 14 or better (equivalent to ISO 1328 class 3).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B004</strong></td><td>Hot “isostatic presses”, as follows, and specially designed dies, moulds, components, accessories and controls therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 2B104 and 2B204.<br>a. Having a controlled thermal environment within the closed cavity and possessing a chamber cavity with an inside diameter of 406 mm or more; and<br>b. Having:<br>1. A maximum working pressure exceeding 207 MPa;<br>2. A controlled thermal environment exceeding 1,773 K (1,500°C); or<br>3. A facility for hydrocarbon impregnation and removal of resultant gaseous degradation products;<br>Technical Note: The inside chamber dimension is that of the chamber in which both the working temperature and the working pressure are achieved and does not include fixtures. That dimension will be the smaller of either the inside diameter of the pressure chamber or the inside diameter of the insulated furnace chamber, depending on which of the two chambers is located inside the other.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B005</strong></td><td>Equipment specially designed for the deposition, processing and in-process control of inorganic overlays, coatings and surface modifications, as follows, for non-electronic substrates, by processes shown in the Table and associated Notes following 2E003.d., and specially designed automated handling, positioning, manipulation and control components therefor:<br>a. “Stored programme controlled” chemical vapour deposition (CVD) production equipment with both of the following:<br>1. Process modified for one of the following:<br>a. Pulsating CVD;<br>b. Controlled nucleation thermal decomposition (CNTD); or<br>c. Plasma enhanced or plasma assisted CVD; and<br>2. Either of the following:<br>a. Incorporating high vacuum (equal to or less than 0.01 Pa) rotating seals; or<br>b. Incorporating in situ coating thickness control;<br>b. “Stored programme controlled” ion implantation production equipment having beam currents of 5 mA or more;<br>c. “Stored programme controlled” electron beam physical vapour deposition (EB-PVD) production equipment incorporating:<br>1. Power systems rated for over 80 kW;<br>2. A liquid pool level “laser” control system which regulates precisely the ingots feed rate; and<br>3. A computer controlled rate monitor operating on the principle of photo-luminescence of the ionised atoms in the evaporant stream to control the deposition rate of a coating containing two or more elements;<br>d. “Stored programme controlled” plasma spraying production equipment having either of the following characteristics:<br>1. Operating at reduced pressure controlled atmosphere (equal to or less than 10 kPa measured above and within 300 mm of the gun nozzle exit) in a vacuum chamber capable of evacuation down to 0.01 Pa prior to the spraying process; or<br>2. Incorporating in situ coating thickness control;<br>e. “Stored programme controlled” sputter deposition production equipment capable of current densities of 0.1 mA/mm<sup>2</sup> or higher at a deposition rate of 15 micrometre/hr or more;<br>f. “Stored programme controlled” cathodic arc deposition production equipment incorporating a grid of electromagnets for steering control of the arc spot on the cathode;<br>g. “Stored programme controlled” ion plating production equipment allowing for the in situ measurement of either:<br>1. Coating thickness on the substrate and rate control; or<br>2. Optical characteristics;<br>NOTE: 2B005.g. does not control standard ion plating coating equipment for cutting or machining tools.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B006</strong></td><td>Dimensional inspection or measuring systems or equipment, as follows:<br>a. Computer controlled, “numerically controlled” or “stored programme controlled” dimensional inspection machines, having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Two or more axes; and<br>2. A one dimensional length “measurement uncertainty” equal to or less (better) than (1.25 + L/1,000) micrometre tested with a probe with an “accuracy” of less (better) than 0.2 micrometre (L is the measured length in mm);<br>b. Linear and angular displacement measuring instruments, as follows:<br>1. Linear measuring instruments having any of the following characteristics:<br>a. Non-contact type measuring systems with a “resolution” equal to or less (better) than 0.2 micrometre within a measuring range up to 0.2 mm;<br>b. Linear voltage differential transformer systems with both of the following characteristics:<br>1. “Linearity” equal to or less (better) than 0.1% within a measuring range up to 5 mm; and<br>2. Drift equal to or less (better) than 0.1% per day at a standard ambient test room temperature ±1 K; or<br>c. Measuring systems having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Containing a “laser”; and<br>2. Maintaining, for at least 12 hours, over a temperature range of ±1 K around a standard temperature and at a standard pressure:<br>a. A “resolution” over their full scale of 0.1 micrometre or less (better); and<br>b. A “measurement uncertainty” equal to or less (better) than (0.2 + L/2,000) micrometre (L is the measured length in mm);<br>2. Angular measuring instruments having an “angular position deviation” equal to or less (better) than 0.00025°;<br>NOTE: 2B006.b.2 does not control optical instruments, such as autocollimators, using collimated light to detect angular displacement of a mirror.<br>c. Systems for simultaneous linear-angular inspection of hemishells, having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. “Measurement uncertainty” along any linear axis equal to or less (better) than 3.5 micrometre per 5 mm; and<br>2. “Angular position deviation” equal to or less (better) than 0.02°;<br>d. Equipment for measuring surface irregularities, by measuring optical scatter as a function of angle, with a sensitivity of 0.5 nm or less (better);<br>Technical Notes:<br>1. The probe used in determining the “measurement uncertainty” of a dimensional inspection system shall be as described in VDI/VDE 2617 Parts 2, 3 and 4.<br>2. All measurement values in 2B006 represent permissible positive and negative deviations from the target value, i.e., not total band.<br>1. NOTES: Machine tools which can be used as measuring machines are controlled if they meet or exceed the criteria specified for the machine tool function or the measuring machine function.<br>2. A machine described in 2B006 is controlled if it exceeds the control threshold anywhere within its operating range.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B007</strong></td><td>“Robots”, as follows, and specially designed controllers and “end-effectors” therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 2B207.<br>a. Capable in real time of full three-dimensional image processing or full three-dimensional scene analysis to generate or modify “programmes” or to generate or modify numerical programme data;<br>NOTE: The scene analysis limitation does not include approximation of the third dimension by viewing at a given angle, or limited grey scale interpretation for the perception of depth or texture for the approved tasks (2½D).<br>b. Specially designed to comply with national safety standards applicable to explosive munitions environments; or<br>c. Specially designed or rated as radiation-hardened beyond that necessary to withstand normal industrial (i.e., non-nuclear industry) ionizing radiation.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B008</strong></td><td>Assemblies, units or inserts specially designed for machine tools, or for equipment specified in 2B006 or 2B007, as follows:<br>a. Spindle assemblies, consisting of spindles and bearings as a minimal assembly, with radial (“run out”) or axial (“camming”) axis motion in one revolution of the spindle less (better) than 0.0006 mm TIR;<br>b. Linear position feedback units (e.g., inductive type devices, graduated scales, infrared systems or “laser” systems) having an overall “accuracy” less (better) than (800 + (600 × L × 10<sup>−</sup>sup3;)) nm (L equals the effective length in mm);<br>c. Rotary position feedback units, e.g., inductive type devices, graduated scales, infrared systems or “laser” systems, having an “accuracy” less (better) than 0.00025°;<br>d. Slide way assemblies consisting of a minimal assembly of ways, bed and slide having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. A yaw, pitch or roll of less (better) than 2 seconds of arc TIR (reference: ISO/DIS 230/1) over full travel;<br>2. A horizontal straightness of less (better) than 2 micrometre per 300 mm length; and<br>3. A vertical straightness of less (better) than 2 micrometre per 300 mm length;<br>e. Single point diamond cutting tool inserts, having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. Flawless and chip-free cutting edge when magnified 400 times in any direction;<br>2. Cutting radius from 0.1 to 5 mm inclusive; and<br>3. Cutting radius out-of-roundness less (better) than 0.002 mm TIR.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B009</strong></td><td>Specially designed printed circuit boards with mounted components and “software” therefor, or “compound rotary tables” or “tilting spindles”, capable of upgrading, according to the manufacturer’s specifications, “numerical control” units, machine tools or feed-back devices to or above the levels specified in 2B001 to 2B008.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B104</strong></td><td>Equipment and process controls designed or modified for densification and pyrolysis of structural composite rocket nozzles and reentry vehicle nose tips.<br>NOTE: The only “isostatic presses” and furnaces specified in this item are as follows:<br>a. “Isostatic presses”, other than those specified in 2B004, having all the following characteristics:<br>1. Maximum working pressure of 69 MPa or greater;<br>2. Designed to achieve and maintain a controlled thermal environment of 873 K (600°C) or greater; and<br>3. Possessing a chamber cavity with an inside diameter of 254 mm or greater;<br>b. CVD Furnaces designed or modified for the densification of carbon-carbon composites.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B115</strong></td><td>Flow-forming machines, and specially designed components therefor,which:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO ENTRY 2B215.<br>a. According to the manufacturer’s technical specification, can be equipped with “numerical control” units or a computer control, even when not equipped with such units; and<br>b. With more than two axes which can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”.<br>Technical Note:<br>1. Machines combining the function of spin-forming and flow-forming are for the purpose of this item regarded as flow-forming machines.<br>2. 2B115 does not control machines that are not usable in the production of propulsion components and equipment (e.g. motor cases) for systems specified in 9A007.a.1.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B116</strong></td><td>Vibration test equipment and components therefor, as follows:<br>a. Vibration test systems employing feedback or closed loop techniques and incorporating a digital controller, capable of vibrating a system at 10 g RMS or more over the entire range 20 Hz to 2000 Hz and imparting forces of 50kN (11,250 lbs), measured bare table, or greater;<br>b. Digital controllers, combined with specially designed vibration test software, with a real-time bandwidth greater than 5 kHz designed for use with vibration test systems in (a) above;<br>c. Vibration thrusters (shaker units),with or without associated amplifiers, capable of imparting a force of 50kN (11,250 lbs), measured ‘bare table’, or greater and usable in vibration test systems in (a.) above;<br>d. Test piece support structures and electronic units designed to combine multiple shaker units in a system capable of providing an effective combined force of 50kN, measured bare table, or greater, and usable in vibration systems in (a) above.<br>In 2B116, “bare table” means a flat table, or surface, with no fixture or fittings.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B204</strong></td><td>“Isostatic presses”, other than those in 2B004 or 2B104, capable of achieving a maximum working pressure of 69 MPa or greater and having a chamber cavity with an inside diameter in excess of 152 mm and specially designed dies, moulds and controls therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B207</strong></td><td>“Robots” and “end-effectors”, other than those specified in 2B007,specially designed to comply with national safety standards applicable to handling high explosives (for example, meeting electrical code ratings for high explosives) and specially designed controllers therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B215</strong></td><td>Spin-forming and flow-forming machines, other than those specified in 2B115, and precision rotor-forming mandrels designed to form cylindrical rotors of inside diameter between 75 mm and 400 mm therefor, which:<br>a. According to the manufacturer’s technical specification, can be equipped with “numerical control” units or a computer control; and<br>b. With two or more axes that can be coordinated simultaneously for “contouring control”.<br>Technical Note: The only spin-forming machines controlled by this item are those combining the function of spin-forming and flow-forming.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B225</strong></td><td>Remote manipulators that provide mechanical translation of human operator actions by electrical, hydraulic or mechanical means to an operating arm and terminal fixture that can be used to provide remote actions in radiochemical separation operations and “hot cells”, as follows:<br>a. Having a capability of penetrating 0.6 m or more of cell wall; or<br>b. Having a capability to bridge over the top of a cell wall with a thickness of 0.6 m or more.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B226</strong></td><td>Vacuum or controlled environment (inert gas) induction furnaces capable of operating above 1,123 K (850°C) and having induction coils 600 mm or less in diameter and specially designed power supplies therefor with an output rating of 5 kW or more.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 3B.<br>NOTE: This entry does not control furnaces designed for the processing of semiconductor wafers.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B227</strong></td><td>Vacuum and controlled atmosphere metallurgical melting and casting furnaces as follows; and specially configured computer control and monitoring systems therefor:<br>a. Electron beam melting and plasma atomization and melting furnaces, with a power of 50 kW or greater, capable of operating with melting temperatures above 1,473 K (1,200°C).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B228</strong></td><td>Rotor fabrication and assembly equipment and bellows-forming mandrels and dies, as follows:<br>a. Rotor assembly equipment for assembly of gas centrifuge rotor tube sections, baffles and end caps, including associated precision mandrels, clamps and shrink fit machines.<br>b. Rotor straightening equipment for alignment of gas centrifuge rotor tube sections to a common axis.<br>Technical Note: Normally such equipment will consist of precision measuring probes linked to a computer that subsequently controls the action of, for example, pneumatic rams used for aligning the rotor tube sections.<br>c. Bellows-forming mandrels and dies for producing single-convolution bellows (bellows made of high-strength aluminium alloys, maraging steel or high strength filamentary materials). The bellows have all of the following dimensions:<br>1. 75 mm to 400 mm inside diameter;<br>2. 12.7 mm or more in length; and<br>3. Single convolution depth more than 2 mm.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B229</strong></td><td>Centrifugal multiplane balancing machines, fixed or portable, horizontal or vertical, as follows:<br>a. Centrifugal balancing machines designed for balancing flexible rotors having a length of 600 mm or more and having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. A swing or journal diameter of 75 mm or more;<br>2. Mass capability of from 0.9 to 23 kg ; and<br>3. Capable of balancing speed of revolution more than 5,000 rpm;<br>b. Centrifugal balancing machines designed for balancing hollow cylindrical rotor components and having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. A journal diameter of 75 mm or more;<br>2. Mass capability of from 0.9 to 23 kg;<br>3. Capable of balancing to a residual imbalance of 0.01 kg mm/kg per plane or better; and<br>4. Belt drive type.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B230</strong></td><td>Instruments capable of measuring pressures up to 13 kPa to an accuracy of better than 1% (full-scale), with corrosion-resistant pressure-sensing elements constructed of nickel, nickel alloys, phosphor bronze, stainless steel, aluminium or aluminium alloys.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B231</strong></td><td>Vacuum pumps with an input throat size of 380 mm or greater with a pumping speed of 15,000 litres/s or greater and capable of producing an ultimate vacuum better than 13 mPa.<br>Technical Note: The ultimate vacuum is determined at the input of the pump with the input of the pump blocked off.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>2B232</strong></td><td>Multistage light gas gun or other high-velocity gun systems (coil, electromagnetic, electrothermal or other advanced systems) capable of accelerating projectiles to 2 km/s or greater.</td></tr>

<tr><td><strong>2B352</strong></td><td>Biological equipment, as follows;<br>a. Complete biological containment facilities at P3, P4 containment level;<br>Technical Note: P3 or P4 (BL3, BL4, L3, L4) containment levels are as specified in the WHO Laboratory Biosafety manual (Geneva, 1983).<br>b. Fermenters, capable of operation without the propagation of aerosols, having all the following characteristics:<br>1. Capacity of 300 litres or more;<br>2. Double or multiple sealing joints within the steam containment area; and<br>3. Capable of in-situ sterilisation in a closed state;<br>Technical Note: Fermenters include bioreactors, chemostats and continuous-flow systems.<br>c. Centrifugal separators, capable of continuous separation without the propagation of aerosols, having all the following characteristics:<br>1. Flow rate exceeding 100 litres per hour;<br>2. Components of polished stainless steel or titanium;<br>3. Double or multiple sealing joints within the steam containment area; and<br>4. Capable of in-situ steam sterilisation in a closed state;<br>Technical Note: Centrifugal separators include decanters.<br>d. Cross-flow filtration equipment, designed for continuous separation without the propagation of aerosols, having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Equal to or greater than 5 square metres; and<br>2. Capable of in-situ sterilization;<br>e. Steam sterilisable freeze drying equipment with a condenser capacity exceeding 50 kg of ice in 24 hours and less than 1,000 kg of ice in 24 hours;<br>f. Equipment that incorporates or is contained in P3 or P4 containment housing, as follows:<br>1. Independently ventilated protective full or half suits;<br>2. Biological safety cabinets or isolators, which allow manual operations to be performed within, whilst providing an environment equivalent to Class III biological protection;<br>Note: In this entry, isolators include flexible isolators, dry boxes, anaerobic chambers and glove boxes.<br>g. Chambers designed for aerosol challenge testing with pathogenic “microorganisms” or “toxins” and having a capacity of 1m<sup>3</sup> or greater.</td></tr>

Capable of generating or modifying, in “real time processing”, “programmes” or data by using the signals obtained simultaneously by means of at least two detection techniques, such as:<br>a. Machine vision (optical ranging);<br>b. Infrared imaging;<br>c. Acoustical imaging (acoustical ranging);<br>d. Tactile measurement;<br>e. Inertial positioning;<br>f. Force measurement;<br>g. Torque measurement;<br>NOTE: 2D002.a. does not control “software” which only provides rescheduling of functionally identical equipment within “flexible manufacturing units” using pre-stored part programmes and a pre-stored strategy for the distribution of the part programmes.<br>b. “Software” for electronic devices other than those described in 2B001.a. or b., which provides the “numerical control” capability of the equipment specified in 2B001.</td></tr>

For the “development” of interactive graphics as an integrated part in “numerical control” units for preparation or modification of part programmes;<br>2. For the “development” of generators of machine tool instructions (e.g., part programmes) from design data residing inside “numerical control” units;<br>3. For the “development” of integration “software” for incorporation of expert systems for advanced decision support of shop floor operations into “numerical control” units;<br>b. “Technology” for metal-working manufacturing processes, as follows:<br>1. “Technology” for the design of tools, dies or fixtures specially designed for the following processes:<br>a. “Superplastic forming”;<br>b. “Diffusion bonding”;<br>c. “Direct-acting hydraulic pressing”;<br>2. Technical data consisting of process methods or parameters as listed below used to control:<br>a. “Superplastic forming” of aluminium alloys, titanium alloys or “superalloys”:<br>1. Surface preparation;<br>2. Strain rate;<br>3. Temperature;<br>4. Pressure;<br>b. “Diffusion bonding” of “superalloys” or titanium alloys:<br>1. Surface preparation;<br>2. Temperature;<br>3. Pressure;<br>c. “Direct-acting hydraulic pressing” of aluminium alloys or titanium alloys:<br>1. Pressure;<br>2. Cycle time;<br>d. “Hot isostatic densification” of titanium alloys, aluminium alloys or “superalloys”:<br>1. Temperature;<br>2. Pressure;<br>3. Cycle time;<br>c. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of hydraulic stretch-forming machines and dies therefor, for the manufacture of airframe structures;<br>d. “Technology” for:<br>The application of inorganic overlay coatings or inorganic surface modification coatings, specified in column 3 of the following Table;<br>To non-electronic substrates, specified in column 2 of the following Table;<br>By processes specified in column 1 of the following Table and defined in the Technical Note;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2">(* The numbers in parenthesis refer to the Notes following this Table.)</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**TABLE—DEPOSITION TECHNIQUES**

<table>
<thead>
<tr><th><em>1. Coating Process (1)*</em></th><th><em>2. Substrate</em></th><th><em>3. Resultant Coating</em></th></tr>
</thead>
<tfoot>
<tr><td colspan="3">TABLE—DEPOSITION TECHNIQUES—NOTES<br>1. The term ‘coating process’ includes coating repair and refurbishing as well as original coating.<br>2. The term ‘alloyed aluminide coating’ includes single or multiple-step coatings in which an element or elements are deposited prior to or during application of the aluminide coating, even if these elements are deposited by another coating process. It does not, however, include the multiple use of single-step pack cementation processes to achieve alloyed aluminides.<br>3. The term ‘noble metal modified aluminide’ coating includes multiple-step coatings in which the noble metal or noble metals are laid down by some other coating process prior to application of the aluminide coating.<br>4. Mixtures consist of infiltrated material, graded compositions, co-deposits and multilayer deposits and are obtained by one or more of the coating processes specified in the Table.<br>5. MCrAlX refers to a coating alloy where M equals cobalt, iron, nickel or combinations thereof and X equals hafnium, yttrium, silicon, tantalum in any amount or other intentional additions over 0.01 weight percent in various proportions and combinations;<br>except:<br>a. CoCrAlY coatings which contain less than 22 weight percent of chromium, less than 7 weight percent of aluminium and less than 2 weight percent of yttrium;<br>b. CoCrAlY coatings which contain 22 to 24 weight percent of chromium, 10 to 12 weight percent of aluminium and 0.5 to 0.7 weight percent of yttrium; or<br>c. NiCrAlY coatings which contain 21 to 23 weight percent of chromium, 10 to 12 weight percent of aluminium and 0.9 to 1.1 weight percent of yttrium.<br>6. The term ‘aluminium alloys’ refers to alloys having an ultimate tensile strength of 190 MPa or more measured at 293 K (20°C).<br>7. The term ‘corrosion resistant steel’ refers to AISI (American Iron and Steel Institute) 300 series or equivalent national standard steels.<br>8. Refractory metals consist of the following metals and their alloys: niobium (columbium), molybdenum, tungsten and tantalum.<br>9. Sensor window materials, as follows: alumina, silicon, germanium, zinc sulphide, zinc selenide, gallium arsenide and the following metal halides: potassium iodide, potassium fluoride, or sensor window materials of more than 40 mm diameter for thallium bromide and thallium chlorobromide.<br>10. “Technology” for single-step pack cementation of solid airfoils is not specified in Category 2.<br>11. Polymers, as follows: polyimide, polyester, polysulphide, polycarbonates and polyurethanes.<br>12. Modified zirconia refers to additions of other metal oxides, e.g., calcia, magnesia, yttria, hafnia, rare earth oxides, etc., to zirconia in order to stabilise certain crystallographic phases and phase compositions. Thermal barrier coatings made of zirconia, modified with calcia or magnesia by mixing or fusion, are not controlled.<br>13. Titanium alloys refers to aerospace alloys having an ultimate tensile strength of 900 MPa or more measured at 293 K (20°C).<br>14. Low-expansion glasses refers to glasses which have a coefficient of thermal expansion of 1 × 10<sup>−7</sup> K<sup>−1</sup> or less measured at 293 K (20°C).<br>15. Dielectric layers are coatings constructed of multi-layers of insulator materials in which the interference properties of a design composed of materials of various refractive indices are used to reflect, transmit or absorb various wavelength bands. Dielectric layers refers to more than four dielectric layers or dielectric/metal “composite” layers.<br>16. Cemented tungsten carbide does not include cutting and forming tool materials consisting of tungsten carbide/(cobalt, nickel), titanium carbide/(cobalt, nickel), chromium carbide/nickel-chromium and chromium carbide/nickel.<br>TABLE—DEPOSITION TECHNIQUES—TECHNICAL NOTE:<br>Processes specified in Column 1 of the Table are defined as follows:<br>a. Chemical Vapour Deposition (CVD) is an overlay coating or surface modification coating process wherein a metal, alloy, “composite”, dielectric or ceramic is deposited upon a heated substrate. Gaseous reactants are decomposed or combined in the vicinity of a substrate resulting in the deposition of the desired elemental, alloy or compound material on the substrate.<br>N.B.: Energy for this decomposition or chemical reaction process may be provided by the heat of the substrate, a glow discharge plasma, or “laser” irradiation.<br>1. CVD includes the following processes: directed gas flow out-of-pack deposition, pulsating CVD, controlled nucleation thermal decomposition (CNTD), plasma enhanced or plasma assisted CVD processes.<br>2. Pack denotes a substrate immersed in a powder mixture.<br>3. The gaseous reactants used in the out-of-pack process are produced using the same basic reactions and parameters as the pack cementation process, except: that the substrate to be coated is not in contact with the powder mixture.<br>b. Thermal Evaporation-Physical Vapour Deposition (TE-PVD) is an overlay coating process conducted in a vacuum with a pressure less than 0.1 Pa wherein a source of thermal energy is used to vaporize the coating material. This process results in the condensation, or deposition, of the evaporated species onto appropriately positioned substrates.<br>The addition of gases to the vacuum chamber during the coating process to synthesize compound coatings is an ordinary modification of the process.<br>The use of ion or electron beams, or plasma, to activate or assist the coating’s deposition is also a common modification in this technique. The use of monitors to provide in-process measurement of optical characteristics and thickness of coatings can be a feature of these processes.<br>Specific TE-PVD processes are as follows:<br>1. Electron Beam PVD uses an electron beam to heat and evaporate the material which forms the coating;<br>2. Resistive Heating PVD employs electrically resistive heating sources capable of producing a controlled and uniform flux of evaporated coating species;<br>3. “Laser” Evaporation uses either pulsed or continuous wave “laser” beams to heat the material which forms the coating;<br>4. Cathodic Arc Deposition employs a consumable cathode of the material which forms the coating and has an arc discharge established on the surface by a momentary contact of a ground trigger. Controlled motion of arcing erodes the cathode surface creating a highly ionized plasma. The anode can be either a cone attached to the periphery of the cathode, through an insulator, or the chamber. Substrate biasing is used for non line-of-sight deposition.<br>N.B.: This definition does not include random cathodic arc deposition with non-biased substrates.<br>c. Ion Plating is a special modification of a general TE-PVD process in which a plasma or an ion source is used to ionize the species to be deposited, and a negative bias is applied to the substrate in order to facilitate the extraction of the species to be deposited from the plasma. The introduction of reactive species, evaporation of solids within the process chamber, and the use of monitors to provide in-process measurement of optical characteristics and thicknesses of coatings are ordinary modifications of the process.<br>d. Pack Cementation is a surface modification coating or overlay coating process wherein a substrate is immersed in a powder mixture (a pack), that consists of:<br>1. The metallic powders that are to be deposited (usually aluminium, chromium, silicon or combinations thereof);<br>2. An activator (normally a halide salt); and<br>3. An inert powder, most frequently alumina.<br>The substrate and powder mixture is contained within a retort which is heated to between 1,030 K (757°C) and 1,375 K (1,102°C) for sufficient time to deposit the coating.<br>e. Plasma Spraying is an overlay coating process wherein a gun (spray torch) which produces and controls a plasma accepts powder or wire coating materials, melts them and propels them towards a substrate, whereon an integrally bonded coating is formed. Plasma spraying constitutes either low pressure plasma spraying or high velocity plasma spraying carried out underwater.<br>1. N.B.: Low pressure means less than ambient atmospheric pressure.<br>2. High velocity refers to nozzle-exit gas velocity exceeding 750 m/s calculated at 293 K (20°C) at 0.1 MPa.<br>f. Slurry Deposition is a surface modification coating or overlay coating process wherein a metallic or ceramic powder with an organic binder is suspended in a liquid and is applied to a substrate by either spraying, dipping or painting, subsequent air or oven drying, and heat treatment to obtain the desired coating.<br>g. Sputter Deposition is an overlay coating process based on a momentum transfer phenomenon, wherein positive ions are accelerated by an electric field towards the surface of a target (coating material). The kinetic energy of the impacting ions is sufficient to cause target surface atoms to be released and deposited on an appropriately positioned substrate.<br>1. N.B.: The Table refers only to triode, magnetron or reactive sputter deposition which is used to increase adhesion of the coating and rate of deposition and to radio frequency (RF) augmented sputter deposition used to permit vapourisation of non-metallic coating materials.<br>2. Low-energy ion beams (less than 5 keV) can be used to activate the deposition.<br>h. Ion Implantation is a surface modification coating process in which the element to be alloyed is ionized, accelerated through a potential gradient and implanted into the surface region of the substrate. This includes processes in which ion implantation is performed simultaneously with electron beam physical vapour deposition or sputter deposition.</td></tr>
</tfoot>

<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 3— ELECTRONICS</th></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A</strong></td><td>NOTES:<br>1. The control status of equipment, devices and components described in 3A001 or 3A002, other than those described in 3A001.a.3. to 10. or 3A001.a.12., which are specially designed or which have the same functional characteristics as other equipment, are determined by the control status of the other equipment.<br>2. The control status of integrated circuits described in 3A001.a.3. to 9. or 3A001.a.12., which are unalterably programmed or designed for a specific function, is determined by the control status of the other equipment.<br>N.B.: When the manufacturer or applicant cannot determine the control status of the other equipment, the control status is determined in 3A001.a.3. to 9. or 3A001.a.12.<br>If the integrated circuit is a silicon-based “microcomputer microcircuits” or microcontroller microcircuit described in 3A001.a.3. having an operand (data) word length of 8 bit or less, the control status of the integrated circuit is determined in 3A001.a.3.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A001</strong></td><td>Electronic devices and components:<br>1. General purpose integrated circuits, as follows:<br>NOTES:<br>1. The control status of wafers (finished or unfinished), in which the function has been determined, is to be evaluated against the parameters of 3A001.a.<br>2. Integrated circuits include the following types:<br>“Monolithic integrated circuits”;<br>“Hybrid integrated circuits”;<br>“Multichip integrated circuits”;<br>“Film type integrated circuits”, including silicon-on-sapphire integrated circuits;<br>“Optical integrated circuits”.<br>1. Integrated circuits, designed or rated as radiation hardened to withstand either of the following:<br>a. A total dose of 5 × 10<sup>5</sup> rads(Si) or higher; or<br>b. A dose rate upset of 5 × 10<sup>8</sup> rads(Si)/s or higher;<br>2. Integrated circuits described in 3A001.a.3. to 10. or 3A001.a.12., as follows:<br>a. Rated for operation at an ambient temperature above 398 K (125°C);<br>b. Rated for operation at an ambient temperature below 218 K (−55°C); or<br>c. Rated for operation over the entire ambient temperature range from 218 K (minus;55°C) to 398 K (125°C);<br>NOTE: 3A001.a.2. does not apply to integrated circuits for civil automobile or railway train applications.<br>3. “Microprocessor microcircuits”, “microcomputer microcircuits” and microcontroller microcircuits, having any of the following:<br>NOTE: 3A001.a.3. includes digital signal processors, digital array processors and digital coprocessors.<br>a. An arithmetic logic unit with an access width of 32 bit or more and a “composite theoretical performance” (CTP) of 80 million theoretical operations per second (Mtops) or more;<br>b. Manufactured from a compound semiconductor and operating at a clock frequency exceeding 40 MHz; or<br>c. More than one data or instruction bus or serial communication port for external interconnection in a parallel processor with a transfer rate exceeding 2.5 Mbyte/s;<br>4. Electrically erasable programmable read-only memories (EEPROMs), static random-access memories (SRAMs) and storage integrated circuits manufactured from a compound semiconductor, as follows:<br>a. EEPROMs with a storage capacity:<br>1. Exceeding 16 Mbit per package for flash memory types; or<br>2. Exceeding either of the following limits for all other EEPROM types:<br>a. 4 Mbit per package; or<br>b. 1 Mbit per package and having a maximum access time of less than 80 ns;<br>b. SRAMs with a storage capacity:<br>1. Exceeding 4 Mbit per package; or<br>2. Exceeding 1 Mbit per package and having a maximum access time of less than 20 ns;<br>c. Storage integrated circuits manufactured from a compound semiconductor;<br>5. Analogue-to-digital and digital-to-analogue converter integrated circuits, as follows:<br>a. Analogue-to-digital converters having any of the following:<br>1. A resolution of 8 bit or more, but less than 12 bit, with a total conversion time to maximum resolution of less than 10 ns;<br>2. A resolution of 12 bit with a total conversion time to maximum resolution of less than 200 ns; or<br>3. A resolution of more than 12 bit with a total conversion time to maximum resolution of less than 2 microseconds;<br>b. Digital-to-analogue converters with a resolution of 12 bit or more, and a “settling time” of less than 10 ns;<br>6. Electro-optical or “optical integrated circuits” for “signal processing” having all of the following:<br>a. One or more internal “laser” diodes;<br>b. One or more internal light detecting elements; and<br>c. Optical waveguides;<br>7. Field programmable gate arrays having either of the following:<br>a. An equivalent usable gate count of more than 30,000 (2 input gates); or<br>b. A typical “basic gate propagation delay time” of less than 0.4 ns;<br>8. Field programmable logic arrays having either of the following:<br>a. An equivalent usable gate count of more than 30,000 (2 input gates); or<br>b. A toggle frequency exceeding 133 MHz;<br>9. Neural network integrated circuits;<br>10. Custom integrated circuits for which either the function is unknown, or the control status of the equipment in which the integrated circuits will be used is unknown to the manufacturer, having any of the following:<br>a. More than 144 terminals;<br>b. A typical “basic gate propagation delay time” of less than 0.4 ns; or<br>c. An operating frequency exceeding 3 GHz;<br>11. Digital integrated circuits, other than those described in 3A001.a.3. to 10. or 3A001.a.12., based upon any compound semiconductor and having either of the following:<br>a. An equivalent gate count of more than 300 (2 input gates); or<br>b. A toggle frequency exceeding 1.2 GHz;<br>12. Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) processors having any of the following:<br>a. A rated execution time for a 1024 point complex FFT of less than 1 ms;<br>b. A rated execution time for an N-point complex FFT of other than 1,024 points of less than N log<sub>2</sub> N/10,240 ms, where N is the number of points; or<br>c. A butterfly throughput of more than 5.12 MHz;<br>2. Microwave or millimetre wave devices:<br>1. Electronic vacuum tubes and cathodes, as follows:<br>NOTES:<br>1. For frequency agile magnetron tubes, see the Military Goods Controls.<br>2. 3A001.b.1. does not specify tubes designed or rated to operate in the Standard Civil Telecommunications Bands at frequencies not exceeding 31 GHz.<br>a. Travelling wave tubes, pulsed or continuous wave, as follows:<br>1. Operating at frequencies higher than 31 GHz;<br>2. Having a cathode heater element with a turn on time to rated RF power of less than 3 seconds;<br>3. Coupled cavity tubes, or derivatives thereof with an “instantaneous bandwidth” of more than 7% or a peak power exceeding 2.5 kW;<br>4. Helix tubes, or derivatives thereof, with any of the following:<br>a. An “instantaneous bandwidth” of more than one octave, and average power (expressed in kW) times frequency (expressed in GHz) of more than 0.5;<br>b. An “instantaneous bandwidth” of one octave or less, and average power (expressed in kW) times frequency (expressed in GHz) of more than 1; or<br>c. “Space qualified”;<br>b. Crossed-field amplifier tubes with a gain of more than 17dB;<br>c. Impregnated cathodes for electronic tubes, with either of the following:<br>1. Having a turn on time to rated emission of less than 3 seconds; or<br>2. Producing a continuous emission current density at rated operating conditions exceeding 5 A/cm<sup>2</sup>;<br>2. Microwave integrated circuits or modules containing “monolithic integrated circuits” operating at frequencies exceeding 3 GHz;<br>NOTE: 3A001.b.2. does not specify circuits or modules for equipment designed or rated to operate in the Standard Civil Telecommunications Bands at frequencies not exceeding 31 GHz.<br>3. Microwave transistors rated for operation at frequencies exceeding 31 GHz;<br>4. Microwave solid state amplifiers, as follows:<br>a. Operating at frequencies exceeding 10.5 GHz and having an “instantaneous bandwidth” of more than half an octave;<br>b. Operating at frequencies exceeding 31 GHz;<br>5. Electronically or magnetically tunable band-pass or band-stop filters having more than 5 tunable resonators capable of tuning across a 1.5:1 frequency band (f<sub>max</sub>/f<sub>min</sub>) in less than 10 microseconds with:<br>a. A band-pass bandwidth of more than 0.5% of centre frequency; or<br>b. A band-stop bandwidth of less than 0.5% of centre frequency;<br>6. Microwave assemblies capable of operating at frequencies exceeding 31 GHz;<br>7. Mixers and converters designed to extend the frequency range of equipment described in 3A002.c., 3A002.e. or 3A002.f. beyond the limits stated therein;<br>3. Acoustic wave devices, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:<br>1. Surface acoustic wave and surface skimming (shallow bulk) acoustic wave devices (i.e., “signal processing” devices employing elastic waves in materials), having any of the following:<br>a. A carrier frequency exceeding 2.5 GHz;<br>b. A carrier frequency of 2.5 GHz or less, and:<br>1. A frequency side-lobe rejection exceeding 55 dB;<br>2. A product of the maximum delay time and the bandwidth (time in microseconds and bandwidth in MHz) of more than 100; or<br>3. A dispersive delay of more than 10 microseconds; or<br>c. A carrier frequency exceeding 1 GHz and a bandwidth of 250 MHz or more;<br>2. Bulk (volume) acoustic wave devices (i.e., “signal processing” devices employing elastic waves) which permit direct processing of signals at frequencies exceeding 1 GHz;<br>3. Acoustic-optic “signal processing” devices employing interaction between acoustic waves (bulk wave or surface wave) and light waves which permit the direct processing of signals or images, including spectral analysis, correlation or convolution;<br>4. Electronic devices or circuits containing components, manufactured from “superconductive” materials specially designed for operation at temperatures below the “critical temperature” of at least one of the “superconductive” constituents, with any of the following:<br>1. Electromagnetic amplification:<br>a. At frequencies equal to or less than 31 GHz with a noise figure of less than 0.5 dB; or<br>b. At frequencies exceeding 31 GHz;<br>2. Current switching for digital circuits using “superconductive” gates with a product of delay time per gate (in seconds) and power dissipation per gate (in watts) of less than 10<sup>−14</sup> J; or<br>3. Frequency selection at all frequencies using resonant circuits with Q-values exceeding 10,000;<br>5. High energy devices, as follows:<br>1. Batteries, as follows:<br>NOTE: 3A001.e.1. does not specify batteries with volumes equal to or less than 27 cm<sup>3</sup> (e.g., standard C-cells or R14 batteries).<br>a. Primary cells and batteries having an energy density exceeding 480 Wh/kg and rated for operation in the temperature range from below 243 K (−30°C) to above 343 K (70°C);<br>b. Rechargeable cells and batteries having an energy density exceeding 150 Wh/kg after 75 charge/discharge cycles at a discharge current equal to C/5 hours (C being the nominal capacity in ampere hours) when operating in the temperature range from below 253 K (−20°C) to above 333 K (60°C);<br>Technical Note: Energy density is obtained by multiplying the average power in watts (average voltage in volts times average current in amperes) by the duration of the discharge in hours to 75% of the open circuit voltage divided by the total mass of the cell (or battery) in kg.<br>c. “Space qualified” and radiation hardened photovoltaic arrays with a specific power exceeding 160 W/m<sup>2</sup> at an operating temperature of 301 K (28°C) under a tungsten illumination of 1 kW/m<sup>2</sup> at 2,800 K (2,527°C);<br>2. High energy storage capacitors, as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 3A201.a.<br>a. Capacitors with a repetition rate of less than 10 Hz (single shot capacitors) having all of the following:<br>1. A voltage rating equal to or more than 5 kV;<br>2. An energy density equal to or more than 250 J/kg; and<br>3. A total energy equal to or more than 25 kJ;<br>b. Capacitors with a repetition rate of 10 Hz or more (repetition rated capacitors) having all of the following:<br>1. A voltage rating equal to or more than 5 kV;<br>2. An energy density equal to or more than 50 J/kg;<br>3. A total energy equal to or more than 100 J; and<br>4. A charge/discharge cycle life equal to or more than 10,000;<br>3. “Superconductive” electromagnets or solenoids specially designed to be fully charged or discharged in less than one second, having all of the following:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 3A201.b.<br>a. Energy delivered during the discharge exceeding 10 kJ in the first second;<br>b. Inner diameter of the current carrying windings of more than 250 mm; and<br>c. Rated for a magnetic induction of more than 8 T or “overall current density” in the winding of more than 300 A/mm<sup>2</sup>;<br>NOTE: 3A001.e.3. does not specify “superconductive” electromagnets or solenoids specially designed for Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) medical equipment.<br>4. Circuits or systems for electromagnetic energy storage, containing components manufactured from “superconductive” materials specially designed for operation at temperatures below the “critical temperature” of at least one of their “superconductive” constituents, having all of the following:<br>a. Resonant operating frequencies exceeding 1 MHz;<br>b. A stored energy density of 1 MJ/m<sup>3</sup> or more; and<br>c. A discharge time of less than 1 ms;<br>5. Flash discharge type X-ray systems, and tubes therefor, having all of the following:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 3A201.c.<br>a. A peak power exceeding 500 MW;<br>b. An output voltage exceeding 500 kV; and<br>c. A pulse width of less than 0.2 microsecond;<br>6. Rotary input type shaft absolute position encoders having either of the following:<br>1. A resolution of better than 1 part in 265,000 (18 bit resolution) of full scale; or<br>2. An accuracy better than ± 2.5 seconds of arc.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A002</strong></td><td>General purpose electronic equipment:<br>a. Recording equipment, as follows, and specially designed test tape therefor:<br>1. Analogue instrumentation magnetic tape recorders, including those permitting the recording of digital signals (e.g., using a high density digital recording (HDDR) module), having any of the following:<br>a. A bandwidth exceeding 4 MHz per electronic channel or track;<br>b. A bandwidth exceeding 2 MHz per electronic channel or track and having more than 42 tracks; or<br>c. A time displacement (base) error, measured in accordance with applicable Inter Range Instrumentation Group (IRIG) or Electronic Industries Association (EIA) documents, of less than ± 0.1 microsecond;<br>2. Digital video magnetic tape recorders having a maximum digital interface transfer rate exceeding 180 Mbit/s;<br>except:<br>Those specially designed for television recording using a signal format standardized or recommended by the International Radio Consultative Committee (CCIR) or the International Technical Commission (IEC) for civil television applications;<br>3. Digital instrumentation magnetic tape data recorders employing helical scan techniques or fixed head techniques, having either of the following characteristics:<br>a. A maximum digital interface transfer rate exceeding 175 Mbit/s; or<br>b. “Space qualified”;<br>NOTE: 3A002.a.3. does not specify analogue magnetic tape recorders equipped with HDDR conversion electronics and configured to record only digital data.<br>c. Equipment, with a maximum digital interface transfer rate exceeding 175 Mbit/s, designed to convert digital video magnetic tape recorders for use as digital instrumentation data recorders;<br>d. Waveform digitisers and transient recorders with both of the following:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 3A202<br>a. Digitising rates equal to or more than 200 million samples per second and a resolution of 10 bits or more; and<br>b. A continuous throughput of 2 Gbits/s or more;<br>Technical Note: For those instruments with a parallel bus architecture, the continuous throughput rate is the highest word rate multiplied by the number of bits in a word. In 3A002.a.5., “Continuous throughput” means the fastest data rate the instrument can output to mass storage without the loss of any information whilst sustaining the sampling rate and analogue-to-digital conversion;<br>4. “Frequency synthesiser” “electronic assemblies” having a “frequency switching time” from one selected frequency to another of less than 1 ms;<br>5. “Signal analysers”, as follows:<br>1. Capable of analysing frequencies exceeding 31 GHz;<br>2. “Dynamic signal analysers” with a “real-time bandwidth” exceeding 25.6 kHz;<br>except:<br>Those using only constant percentage bandwidth filters (also known as octave or fractional octave filters);<br>6. Frequency synthesised signal generators producing output frequencies, the accuracy and short term and long term stability of which are controlled, derived from or disciplined by the internal master frequency, and having any of the following:<br>1. A maximum synthesised frequency exceeding 31 GHz;<br>2. A “frequency switching time” from one selected frequency to another of less than 1 ms; or<br>3. A single sideband (SSB) phase noise better than—(126+20log<sub>10</sub>F−20log<sub>10</sub>f) in dBc/Hz, where F is the off-set from the operating frequency in Hz and f is the operating frequency in MHz;<br>NOTE: 3A002.d. does not specify equipment in which the output frequency is either produced by the addition or subtraction of two or more crystal oscillator frequencies, or by an addition or subtraction followed by a multiplication of the result.<br>7. Network analysers with a maximum operating frequency exceeding 31 GHz;<br>NOTE: 3A002.e. does not specify “swept frequency network analysers” with a maximum operating frequency not exceeding 40 GHz and which do not contain a data bus for remote control interfacing.<br>8. Microwave test receivers with both of the following:<br>1. A maximum operating frequency exceeding 31 GHz; and<br>2. Capable of measuring amplitude and phase simultaneously;<br>9. Atomic frequency standards having either of the following characteristics:<br>1. Long term stability (aging) less (better) than 1×10<sup>−11</sup>/month; or<br>NOTE: 3A002.g.1. does not specify non-“space qualified” rubidium standards.<br>2. “Space qualified”;<br>10. Emulators for microcircuits specified in 3A001.a.3. or 3A001.a.9.;<br>NOTE: 3A002.h. does not specify emulators designed for a “family” which contains at least one device not specified in 3A001.a.3. or 3A001.a.9.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A101</strong></td><td>Electronic equipment, devices and components, other than those specified in 3A001, as follows:<br>a. Analog-to-digital converters, usable in “missiles”, designed to meet military specifications for ruggedized equipment.<br>b. Accelerators capable of delivering electromagnetic radiation produced by bremsstrahlung from accelerated electrons of 2 Mev or greater, and systems containing those accelerators.<br>NOTE: 3A101.b. above does not specify equipment specially designed for medical purposes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A201</strong></td><td>Electronic components, other than those specified in 3A001, as follows;<br>a. Capacitors with the following characteristics:<br>1. Voltage rating greater than 1.4 kV, energy storage greater than 10 J, capacitance greater than 0.5 uF and series inductance less than 50 nH; or<br>2. Voltage rating greater than 750 V, capacitance greater than 0.25 uF and series inductance less than 10 nH;<br>b. Superconducting solenoidal electromagnets with all of the following characteristics:<br>1. Capable of creating magnetic fields of more than 2 teslas (20 kilogauss);<br>2. With an L/D ratio (length divided by inner diameter) greater than 2;<br>3. With an inner diameter of more than 300 mm; and<br>4. With a magnetic field uniform to better than 1% over the central 50% of the inner volume;<br>NOTE: 3A201.b. does not specify magnets specially designed for and exported as parts of medical nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) imaging systems. The phrase “as part of” does not necessarily mean physical part in the same shipment; separate shipments from different sources are allowed, provided the related export documents clearly specify that the shipments are dispatched “as part of” the imaging systems.<br>c. Flash X-ray generators or pulsed electron accelerators with peak energy of 500 keV or greater, as follows;<br>except:<br>Accelerators that are component parts of devices designed for purposes other than electron beam or X-ray radiation (electron microscopy, for example) and those designed for medical purposes:<br>1. Having an accelerator peak electron energy of 500 keV or greater but less than 25 MeV and with a figure of merit (K) of 0.25 or greater, where K is defined as:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>where<br>V is the peak electron energy in million electron volts and Q is the total accelerated charge in coulombs if the accelerator beam pulse duration is less than or equal to 1 microsecond; if the accelerator beam pulse duration is greater than 1 microsecond, Q is the maximum accelerated charge in 1 microsecond [Q equals the integral of i with respect to t, over the lesser of 1 microsecond or the time duration of the beam pulse (Q = [integral] idt), where i is beam current in amperes and t is time in seconds]; or<br>2. Having an accelerator peak electron energy of 25 MeV or greater and a peak power greater than 50 MW. [Peak power = (peak potential in volts) × (peak beam current in amperes)]<br>a. Technical Notes: Time duration of the beam pulse—In machines, based on microwave accelerating cavities, the time duration of the beam pulse is the lesser of 1 microsecond or the duration of the bunched beam packet resulting from one microwave modulator pulse.<br>b. Peak beam current—In machines based on microwave accelerating cavities, the peak beam current is the average current in the time duration of a bunched beam packet.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A202</strong></td><td>Oscilloscopes and transient recorders other than those specified in 3A002.a.5., as follows; and specially designed components therefor:<br>a. Non-modular analogue oscilloscopes having a bandwidth of 1 GHz or greater;<br>b. Modular analogue oscilloscope systems having either of the following characteristics:<br>1. A mainframe with a bandwidth of 1 GHz or greater; or<br>2. Plug-in modules with an individual bandwidth of 4 GHz or greater;<br>c. Analogue sampling oscilloscopes for the analysis of recurring phenomena with an effective bandwidth greater than 4 GHz;<br>d. Digital oscilloscopes and transient recorders, using analogue-to-digital conversion techniques, capable of storing transients by sequentially sampling single-shot inputs at successive intervals of less than 1 ns (greater than 1 giga-sample per second), digitizing to 8 bits or greater resolution and storing 256 or more samples.<br>NOTE: Specially designed components specified in this item are the following, for analogue oscilloscopes:<br>1. Plug-in units;<br>2. External amplifiers;<br>3. Pre-amplifiers;<br>4. Sampling devices;<br>5. Cathode ray tubes.<br>Technical Note: ‘Bandwidth’ is defined as the band of frequencies over which the deflection on the cathode ray tube does not fall below 70.7% of that at the maximum point measured with a constant input voltage to the oscilloscope amplifier.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A225</strong></td><td>Frequency changers (also known as converters or inverters) or generators, other than those specified in 0B001.c.11., having all of the following characteristics:<br>a. A multiphase output capable of providing a power of 40 W or more;<br>b. Capable of operating in the frequency range between 600 and 2,000 Hz;<br>c. Total harmonic distortion below 10%; and<br>d. Frequency control better than 0.1%.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A226</strong></td><td>Direct current high-power supplies capable of continuously producing, over a time period of 8 hours, 100 V or greater with current output of 500 A or greater and with current or voltage regulation better than 0.1%.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A227</strong></td><td>High-voltage direct current power supplies capable of continuously producing, over a time period of 8 hours, 20,000 V or greater with current output of 1 A or greater and with current or voltage regulation better than 0.1%.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A228</strong></td><td>Switching devices, as follows:<br>a. Cold-cathode tubes (including gas krytron tubes and vacuum sprytron tubes), whether gas filled or not, operating similarly to a spark gap, containing three or more electrodes, and having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. Anode peak voltage rating of 2,500 V or more;<br>2. Anode peak current rating of 100 A or more; and<br>3. Anode delay time of 10 microsecond or less;<br>b. Triggered spark-gaps having an anode delay time of 15 microsecond or less and rated for a peak current of 500 A or more;<br>c. Modules or assemblies with a fast switching function having all of the following characteristics:<br>1. Anode peak voltage rating greater than 2,000 V;<br>2. Anode peak current rating of 500 A or more; and<br>3. Turn-on time of 1 microsecond or less.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A229</strong></td><td>Firing sets and equivalent high-current pulse generators (for controlled detonators), as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO MILITARY GOODS CONTROLS.<br>a. Explosive detonator firing sets designed to drive multiple controlled detonators specified in 3A232;<br>b. Modular electrical pulse generators (pulsers) designed for portable, mobile or ruggedized use (including xenon flash-lamp drivers) having all the following characteristics:<br>1. Capable of delivering their energy in less than 15 microsecond;<br>2. Having an output greater than 100 A;<br>3. Having a rise time of less than 10 microsecond into loads of less than 40 ohms (rise time is the time interval from 10% to 90% current amplitude when driving a resistive load);<br>4. Enclosed in a dust-tight enclosure;<br>5. No dimension greater than 254 mm;<br>6. Weight less than 25 kg; and<br>7. Specified for use over an extended temperature range (223 K [−50° C] to 373 K [100° C]) or specified as suitable for aerospace use.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A230</strong></td><td>High-speed pulse generators with output voltages greater than 6 volts into a less than 55 ohm resistive load, and with pulse transition times less than 500 picoseconds.<br>Technical Note: In this item, ‘pulse transition time’ is defined as the time interval between 10% and 90% voltage amplitude.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A231</strong></td><td>Neutron generator systems, including tubes, designed for operation without an external vacuum system and utilizing electrostatic acceleration to induce a tritium-deuterium nuclear reaction.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A232</strong></td><td>Detonators and multipoint initiation systems, as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO MILITARY GOODS CONTROLS.<br>a. Electrically driven explosive detonators, the following:<br>1. Exploding bridge (EB);<br>2. Exploding bridge wire (EBW);<br>3. Slapper;<br>4. Exploding foil initiators (EFI);<br>b. Arrangements using single or multiple detonators designed to nearly simultaneously initiate an explosive surface (over greater than 5000 mm<sup>2</sup>) from a single firing signal (with an initiation timing spread over the surface of less than 2.5 microseconds).<br>NOTE: This item does not specify detonators using only primary explosives, such as lead azide.<br>Technical Note: The detonators of concern all utilise a small electrical conductor (bridge, bridge wire or foil) that explosively vapourises when a fast, high-current electrical pulse is passed through it. In nonslapper types, the exploding conductor starts a chemical detonation in a contacting high-explosive material such as PETN (Pentaerythritoltetranitrate). In slapper detonators, the explosive vapourisation of the electrical conductor drives a flyer or slapper across a gap and the impact of the slapper on an explosive starts a chemical detonation. The slapper in some designs is driven by a magnetic force. The term exploding foil detonator may refer to either an EB or a slapper-type detonator. Also, the word initiator is sometimes used in place of the word detonator.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3A233</strong></td><td>Mass spectrometers, other than those specified in 0B002.g., capable of measuring ions of 230 atomic mass units or greater and having a resolution of better than 2 parts in 230, as follows, and ion sources therefor:<br>a. Inductively coupled plasma mass spectrometers (ICP/MS);<br>b. Glow discharge mass spectrometers (GDMS);<br>c. Thermal ionization mass spectrometers (TIMS);<br>d. Electron bombardment mass spectrometers which have a source chamber constructed from, lined with or plated with materials resistant to UF<sub>6</sub>;<br>e. Molecular beam mass spectrometers as follows:<br>1. Which have a source chamber constructed from, lined with or plated with stainless steel or molybdenum and have a cold trap capable of cooling to 193 K (−80°C) or less; or<br>2. Which have a source chamber constructed from, lined with or plated with materials resistant to UF<sub>6</sub>; or<br>f. Mass spectrometers equipped with a microfluorination ion source designed for use with actinides or actinide fluorides.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B</strong></td><td>Equipment for the manufacture or testing of semiconductor devices or materials, as follows, and specially designed components and accessories therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B001</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” equipment for epitaxial growth, as follows:<br>a. Capable of producing a layer thickness uniform to less than ±2.5% across a distance of 75 mm or more;<br>b. Metal organic chemical vapour deposition (MOCVD) reactors specially designed for compound semiconductor crystal growth by the chemical reaction between materials specified in 3C003 or 3C004;<br>c. Molecular beam epitaxial growth equipment using gas sources.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B002</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” equipment designed for ion implantation, having any of the following:<br>a. An accelerating voltage exceeding 200 keV;<br>b. Specially designed and optimized to operate at an accelerating voltage of less than 10 keV;<br>c. Direct write capability; or<br>d. Capable of high energy oxygen implant into a heated semiconductor material “substrate”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B003</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” anisotropic plasma dry etching equipment, as follows:<br>a. With cassette-to-cassette operation and load-locks, and having either of the following:<br>1. Magnetic confinement; or<br>2. Electron cyclotron resonance (ECR);<br>b. Specially designed for equipment specified in 3B005 and having either of the following:<br>1. Magnetic confinement; or<br>2. Electron cyclotron resonance (ECR).</td></tr>
