<tr><td><strong>3B004</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” plasma enhanced CVD equipment, as follows:<br>a. With cassette-to-cassette operation and load-locks, and having either of the following:<br>1. Magnetic confinement; or<br>2. Electron cyclotron resonance (ECR);<br>b. Specially designed for equipment specified in 3B005 and having either of the following:<br>1. Magnetic confinement; or<br>2. Electron cyclotron resonance (ECR).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B005</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” automatic loading multi-chamber central wafer handling systems, having interfaces for wafer input and output, to which more than two pieces of semiconductor processing equipment are to be connected, to form an integrated system in a vacuum environment for sequential multiple wafer processing.<br>NOTE: This item does not specify automatic robotic wafer handling systems not designed to operate in a vacuum environment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B006</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” lithography equipment, as follows:<br>a. Align and expose step and repeat equipment for wafer processing using photo-optical or X-ray methods, having either of the following:<br>1. A light source wavelength shorter than 400nm; or<br>2. Capable of producing a pattern with a minimum resolvable feature size of 0.7 micrometre or less when calculated by the following formula:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>where:<br>‘MRF’ is the minimum resolvable feature size;<br>the ‘K factor’ = 0.7; and<br>‘wavelength’ is the exposure light source wavelength;<br>b. Equipment specially designed for mask making or semiconductor device processing using deflected focussed electron beam, ion beam or “laser” beam, with any of the following:<br>1. A spot size smaller than 0.2 micrometre;<br>2. Capable of producing a pattern with a feature size of less than 1 micrometre; or<br>3. An overlay accuracy of better than ± 0.20 micrometre (3 sigma).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B007</strong></td><td>Masks or reticles, as follows:<br>a. For integrated circuits specified in 3A001;<br>b. Multi-layer masks with a phase shift layer.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3B008</strong></td><td>“Stored programme controlled” test equipment, specially designed for testing semiconductor devices and unencapsulated dice, as follows:<br>a. For testing S-parameters of transistor devices at frequencies exceeding 31 GHz;<br>b. For testing integrated circuits capable of performing functional (truth table) testing at a pattern rate of more than 40 MHz;<br>NOTE: 3B008.b. does not specify test equipment specially designed for testing:<br>1. “Electronic assemblies” or a class of “electronic assemblies” for home or entertainment applications;<br>2. Non-controlled electronic components, “electronic assemblies” or integrated circuits.<br>c. For testing microwave integrated circuits at frequencies exceeding 3 GHz;<br>NOTE: 3B008.c. does not specify test equipment specially designed for testing microwave integrated circuits for equipment designed or rated to operate in the Standard Civil Telecommunication Bands at frequencies not exceeding 31 GHz.<br>d. Electron beam systems designed for operation at or below 3 keV, or “laser” beam systems, for the non-contactive probing of powered-up semiconductor devices, with both of the following:<br>1. Stroboscopic capability with either beam-blanking or detector strobing; and<br>2. An electron spectrometer for voltage measurement with a resolution of less than 0.5 V.<br>NOTE: 3B008.d. does not specify scanning electron microscopes;<br>except:<br>When specially designed and instrumented for the non-contactive probing of powered-up semiconductor devices.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3C</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3C001</strong></td><td>Hetero-epitaxial materials consisting of a “substrate” with stacked epitaxially grown multiple layers of:<br>a. Silicon;<br>b. Germanium; or<br>c. III/V compounds of gallium or indium.<br>Technical Note: III/V compounds are polycrystalline or binary or complex monocrystalline products consisting of elements of groups IIIA and VA of Mendeleyev’s periodic classification table (gallium arsenide, gallium-aluminium arsenide, indium phosphide, etc.).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3C002</strong></td><td>Resist materials, as follows, and “substrates” coated with controlled resists:<br>a. Positive resists for semiconductor lithography specially adjusted (optimised) for use at wavelengths below 370 nm;<br>b. All resists, for use with electron beams or ion beams, with a sensitivity of 0.01 microcoulomb/mm<sup>2</sup> or better;<br>c. All resists, for use with X-rays, with a sensitivity of 2.5 mJ/mm<sup>2</sup> or better;<br>d. All resists optimized for surface imaging technologies, including silylated resists.<br>Technical Note: Silylation techniques are defined as processes incorporating oxidation of the resist surface to enhance performance for both wet and dry developing.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3C003</strong></td><td>Organo-inorganic compounds as follows:<br>a. Organo-metallic compounds of aluminium, gallium or indium, having a purity (metal basis) better than 99.999%;<br>b. Organo-arsenic, organo-antimony and organo-phosphorus compounds having a purity (inorganic element basis) better than 99.999%.<br>NOTE: 3C003 only specifies compounds whose metallic, partly metallic or non-metallic element is directly linked to carbon in the organic part of the molecule.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3C004</strong></td><td>Hydrides of phosphorus, arsenic or antimony, having a purity better than 99.999%, even if diluted in neutral gases.<br>NOTE: 3C004 does not specify hydrides containing 20% molar or more of inert gases or hydrogen.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3D</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3D001</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “development” or “production” of equipment specified in 3A001.b to 3A002.h or 3B.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3D002</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “use” of “stored programme controlled” equipment specified in 3B.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3D003</strong></td><td>Computer-aided-design (CAD) “software” for semiconductor devices or integrated circuits, having any of the following:<br>a. Design rules or circuit verification rules;<br>b. Simulation of the physically laid out circuits; or<br>c. Lithographic processing simulators for design.<br>Technical Note: A lithographic processing simulator is a “software” package used in the design phase to define the sequence of lithographic, etching and deposition steps for translating masking patterns into specific topographical patterns in conductors, dielectrics or semiconductor material.<br>NOTE: 3D003 does not specify “software” specially designed for schematic entry, logic simulation, placing and routing, layout verification or pattern generation tape.<br>N.B.: Libraries, design attributes or associated data for the design of semiconductor devices or integrated circuits are considered as “technology”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3D101</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “use” of equipment specified in 3A101 b.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3E</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3E001</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development” or “production” of equipment or materials specified in 3A, 3B or 3C;<br>NOTE: 3E001 does not specify “technology” for the “development” or “production” of:<br>a. Microwave transistors operating at frequencies below 31 GHz;<br>b. Integrated circuits specified in 3A001.a.3. to 12., having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Using technology of one micrometre or more; and<br>2. Not incorporating multi-layer structures.<br>N.B.: This Note does not preclude the export of multilayer technology for devices incorporating a maximum of two metal layers and two polysilicon layers.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3E002</strong></td><td>Other “technology” for the “development” or “production” of:<br>a. Vacuum microelectronic devices;<br>b. Hetero-structure semiconductor devices such as high electron mobility transistors (HEMT), hetero-bipolar transistors (HBT), quantum well or super lattice devices;<br>c. “Superconductive” electronic devices;<br>d. Substrates of films of diamond for electronic components.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3E101</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “use” of equipment or “software” specified in 3A001.a.1 or 2, 3A101 or 3D101.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3E102</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development” of software specified in 3D101.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>3E201</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “use” of equipment specified in 3A001.e.2, 3A001.e.3, 3A001.e.5, 3A201, 3A202, 3A225 to 3A233.</td></tr>
<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 4— COMPUTERS</th></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>NOTES:<br>1. Computers, related equipment or “software” performing telecommunications or “local area network” functions must also be evaluated against the performance characteristics of Category 5 (Part 1—Telecommunications).<br>N.B.:<br>1. Control units which directly interconnect the buses or channels of central processing units, “main storage” or disk controllers are not regarded as telecommunications equipment described in Category 5 (Part 1—Telecommunications).<br>2. For the control status of “software” which provides routing or switching of “datagram” or “fast select” packets (i.e., packet by packet route selection) or of “software” specially designed for packet switching, see Category 5 (Part 1—Telecommunications).<br>2. Computers, related equipment or “software” performing cryptographic, cryptanalytic, certifiable multi-level security or certifiable user isolation functions, or which limit electromagnetic compatibility (EMC), must also be evaluated against the performance characteristics in Category 5 (Part 2—“Information Security”).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A001</strong></td><td>Electronic computers and related equipment, as follows, and “electronic assemblies” and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 4A101.<br>a. Specially designed to have either of the following characteristics:<br>1. Rated for operation at an ambient temperature below 228 K (−45° C) or above 358 K (85° C); or<br>NOTE: 4A001.a.1. does not apply to computers specially designed for civil automobile or railway train applications.<br>2. Radiation hardened to exceed any of the following specifications:<br>a. Total Dose 5 × 10<sup>5</sup> Rads (Si);<br>b. Dose Rate Upset 5 × 10<sup>8</sup> Rads (Si)/sec; or<br>c. Single Event Upset 1 × 10<sup>−7</sup> Error/bit/day;<br>NOTE: For equipment designed or rated for transient ionising radiation, see the Military Goods Controls.<br>b. Having characteristics or performing functions exceeding the limits in Category 5 (Part 2—“Information Security”).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A002</strong></td><td>“Hybrid computers”, as follows, and “electronic assemblies” and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO ENTRY 4A102.<br>a. Containing “digital computers” specified in 4A003;<br>b. Containing analogue-to-digital converters having any of the following characteristics:<br>1. 32 channels or more; and<br>2. A resolution of 14 bits (plus sign bit) or more with a conversion rate of 200,000 conversions/s or more.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A003</strong></td><td>“Digital computers”, “electronic assemblies”, and related equipment therefor, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:<br>1. NOTES: 4A003 includes vector processors, array processors, digital signal processors, logic processors, and equipment for “image enhancement” or “signal processing”.<br>2. The control status of the “digital computers” or related equipment described in 4A003 is governed by the control status of other equipment or systems provided:<br>a. The “digital computers” or related equipment are essential for the operation of the other equipment or systems;<br>b. The “digital computers” or related equipment are not a “principal element” of the other equipment or systems; and<br>1. N.B.: The control status of “signal processing” or “image enhancement” equipment specially designed for other equipment with functions limited to those required for the other equipment is determined by the control status of the other equipment even if it exceeds the “principal element” criterion.<br>2. For the control status of “digital computers” or related equipment for telecommunications equipment, see Category 5 (Part 1—Telecommunications).<br>c. The “technology” for the “digital computers” and related equipment is specified in 4E.<br>a. Designed or modified for “fault tolerance”;<br>NOTE: For the purposes of 4A003.a, “digital computers” and related equipment are not considered to be designed or modified for “fault tolerance” if they use:<br>1. Error detection or correction algorithms in “main storage”;<br>2. The interconnection of two “digital computers” so that, if the active central processing unit fails, an idling but mirroring central processing unit can continue the system’s functioning;<br>3. The interconnection of two central processing units by data channels or by use of shared storage to permit one central processing unit to perform other work until the second central processing unit fails, at which time the first central processing unit takes over in order to continue the system’s functioning; or<br>4. The synchronisation of two central processing units by “software” so that one central processing unit recognises when the other central processing unit fails and recovers tasks from the failing unit.<br>b. “Digital computers” having a “composite theoretical performance” (CTP) exceeding 260 million theoretical operations per second (Mtops);<br>c. “Electronic assemblies” specially designed or modified to be capable of enhancing performance by aggregation of “computing elements” so that the “Composite theoretical performance” of the aggregation exceeds the limit in 4A003.b.;<br>1. NOTES: 4A003.c. applies only to “electronic assemblies” and programmable interconnections not exceeding the limit in 4A003.b., when shipped as unintegrated “electronic assemblies”. It does not apply to “electronic assemblies” inherently limited by nature of their design for use as related equipment specified in 4A003.d.,e. or f.<br>2. 4A003.c. does not specify “electronic assemblies” specially designed for a product or family of products whose maximum configuration does not exceed the limit of 4A003.b.<br>d. Graphics accelerators or graphics coprocessors exceeding a “3-D Vector Rate” of 1,600,000;<br>e. Equipment performing analogue-to-digital conversions exceeding the limits in 3A001.a.5.;<br>f. Equipment containing “terminal interface equipment” exceeding the limits in 5A001.b.3.<br>NOTE: For the purposes of 4A003.f., “terminal interface equipment” includes “local area network” interfaces, modems and other communications interfaces. “Local area network” interfaces are evaluated as “network access controllers”.<br>g. Equipment specially designed to provide for the external interconnection of “digital computers” or associated equipment which allows communications at data rates exceeding 80 Mbytes/s.<br>NOTE: 4A003.g. does not control internal interconnection equipment (e.g. backplanes, buses) or passive interconnection equipment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A004</strong></td><td>Computers, as follows, and specially designed related equipment, “electronic assemblies” and components therefor:<br>a. “Systolic array computers”;<br>b. “Neural computers”;<br>c. “Optical computers”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A101</strong></td><td>Analogue computers, “digital computers” or digital differential analysers, other than those specified in 4A001.a.1, which are ruggedized and designed or modified for use in systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4A102</strong></td><td>“Hybrid computers” specially designed for modelling, simulation or design integration of systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104.<br>NOTE: This control only applies when the equipment is supplied with software specified in 7D103 or 9D103.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4B</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>None</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4C</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>None</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4D</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>NOTE: The control status of “software” for the “development”, “production”, or “use” of equipment described in other Categories is dealt with in the appropriate Category. The control status of “software” for equipment described in this Category is dealt with herein.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4D001</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed or modified for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment, materials or “software” specified in 4A001 to 4A004, or 4D.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4D002</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed or modified to support “technology” specified in 4E.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4D003</strong></td><td>Specific “software”, as follows:<br>a. Operating system “software”, “software” development tools and compilers specially designed for “multi-data-stream processing” equipment, in “source code”;<br>b. “Expert systems” or “software” for “expert system” inference engines providing both:<br>1. Time dependent rules; and<br>2. Primitives to handle the time characteristics of the rules and the facts;<br>c. “Software” having characteristics or performing functions exceeding the limits in Category 5 (Part 2—“Information Security”);<br>d. Operating systems specially designed for “real time processing” equipment which guarantees a “global interrupt latency time” of less than 20 microseconds.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4E</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4E001</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note, for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment, or “software” specified in 4A or 4D.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>4E002</strong></td><td>Other technology, as follows:<br>a. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of equipment designed for “multi-data-stream processing” where the “composite theoretical performance (CTP)” exceeds 120 Mtops;<br>b. “Technology” “required” for the “development” or “production” of magnetic hard disk drives with a “maximum bit transfer rate” exceeding 47 Mbit/s.</td></tr>
<tr><td>4.</td><td>Technical Note<br>“COMPOSITE THEORETICAL PERFORMANCE” (CTP)<br>Abbreviations used in this Note</td></tr>
<tr><td>CE</td><td>“computing element” (typically an arithmetic logical unit)</td></tr>
<tr><td>FP</td><td>floating point</td></tr>
<tr><td>XP</td><td>fixed point</td></tr>
<tr><td>t</td><td>execution time</td></tr>
<tr><td>XOR</td><td>exclusive OR</td></tr>
<tr><td>CPU</td><td>central processing unit</td></tr>
<tr><td>TP</td><td>theoretical performance (of a single CE)</td></tr>
<tr><td>CTP</td><td>“composite theoretical performance” (multiple CEs)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Mtops</td><td>millions of theoretical operations per second</td></tr>
<tr><td>R</td><td>effective calculating rate</td></tr>
<tr><td>WL</td><td>word length</td></tr>
<tr><td>L</td><td>word length adjustment</td></tr>
<tr><td>*</td><td>multiply</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>Execution time ‘t’ is expressed in microseconds, TP and “CTP” are expressed in millions of theoretical operations per second (Mtops) and WL is expressed in bits.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2">Outline of the CTP calculation method<br>CTP is a measure of computational performance given in Mtops. In calculating the CTP of an aggregation of CEs the following three steps are required:<br>1. Calculate the effective calculating rate R for each CE;<br>2. Apply the word length adjustment (L) to the effective calculating rate (R), resulting in a Theoretical Performance (TP) for each CE;<br>3. If there is more than one CE, combine the TPs resulting in a CTP for the aggregation.<br>Details for these steps are given in the following sections.<br>Note 1: For aggregations of multiple CEs which have both shared and unshared memory subsystems, the calculation of CTP is completed hierarchically, in two steps: first, aggregate the group of CEs sharing memory, second calculate the CTP of the groups using the calculation method for multiple CEs not sharing memory.<br>Note 2: CEs that are limited to input/output and peripheral functions (e.g. disk drive, communication and video display controllers) are not aggregated into the CTP calculation.<br>The following table shows the method of calculating the effective calculating rate (R) for each CE:<br>Step 1: The effective calculating rate R<br><table><thead><tr><th>For CEs implementing<br>Note: Every CE must be evaluated independently</th><th>Effective calculating rate, R</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>XP only</td><td><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a></td></tr><tr><td></td><td>if no add is implemented use:</td></tr><tr><td>(R<sub>xp</sub>)</td><td><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>If neither add nor multiply is implemented use the fastest available arithmetic operation as follows:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>See Notes X &amp; Z</td></tr><tr><td>FP only<br>(R<sub>fp</sub>)</td><td>Max<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>See Notes X &amp; Y</td></tr><tr><td>Both FP and XP<br>(R)</td><td>Calculate both<br>R<sub>xp</sub>, R<sub>fp</sub></td></tr><tr><td>For simple logic processors not implementing any of the specified arithmetic operations.</td><td><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>Where<br>t<sub>log</sub> is the execute time of the XOR, or for logic hardware not implementing the XOR, the fastest simple logic operation.<br>See Notes X &amp; Z</td></tr><tr><td>For special logic processors not using any of the specified arithmetic or logic operations.</td><td><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>Where<br>R’ is the number of results per second, WL is the number of bits upon which the logic operation occurs, and 64 is a factor to normalize to a 64 bit operation.</td></tr></tbody></table><br>Note W:<br>For a pipelined CE capable of executing up to one arithmetic or logic operation every clock cycle after the pipeline is full, a pipelined rate can be established. The effective calculating rate (R) for such a CE is the faster of the pipelined rate or non-pipelined execution rate.<br>Note X:<br>For CEs which perform multiple operations of a specific type in a single cycle (e.g., two additions per cycle or two identical logic operations per cycle), the execution time t is given by:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>CEs which perform different types of arithmetic or logic operations in a single machine cycle are to be treated as multiple separate CEs performing simultaneously (e.g., a CE performing an addition and a multiplication in one cycle is to be treated as two CEs, the first performing an addition in one cycle and the second performing a multiplication in one cycle).<br>If a single CE has both scalar function and vector function, use the shorter execution time value.<br>Note Y:<br>For the CE that does not implement FP add or FP multiply, but that performs FP divide:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>If the CE implements FP reciprocal but not FP add, FP multiply or FP divide, then<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>If none of the specified instructions is implemented, the effective FP rate is 0.<br>Note Z:<br>In simple logic operations, a single instruction performs a single logic manipulation of no more than two operands of given lengths. In complex logic operations, a single instruction performs multiple logic manipulations to produce one or more results from two or more operands.<br>Rates should be calculated for all supported operand lengths considering both pipelined operations (if supported), and non-pipelined operations using the fastest executing instruction for each operand length based on:<br>1. Pipelined or register-to-register operations. Exclude extraordinarily short execution times generated for operations on a predetermined operand or operands (for example, multiplication by 0 or 1). If no register-to-register operations are implemented, continue with (2).<br>2. The faster of register-to-memory or memory-to-register operations; if these also do not exist, then continue with (3).<br>3. Memory-to-memory.<br>In each case above, use the shortest execution time certified by the manufacturer.<br>Step 2: TP for each supported operand length WL<br>Adjust the effective rate R (or R') by the word length adjustment L as follows:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>where<br>L<br>(1/3 + WL/96)<br>Note: The word length WL used in these calculations is the operand length in bits. (If an operation uses operands of different lengths, select the largest word length.) The combination of a mantissa ALU and an exponent ALU of a floating point processor or unit is considered to be one CE with a Word Length (WL) equal to the number of bits in the data representation (typically 32 or 64) for purposes of the CTP calculation.<br>This adjustment is not applied to specialized logic processors which do not use XOR instructions. In this case TP = R.<br>Select the maximum resulting value of TP for:<br>Each XP-only CE (R<sub>xp</sub>);<br>Each FP-only CE (R<sub>fp</sub>);<br>Each combined FP and XP CE (R);<br>Each simple logic processor not implementing any of the specified arithmetic operations; and<br>Each special logic processor not using any of the specified arithmetic or logic operations.<br>Step 3: CTP for aggregations of CEs, including CPUs<br>For a CPU with a single CE,<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>(for CEs performing both fixed and floating point operations<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>)<br>CTP for aggregations of multiple CEs operating simultaneously is calculated as follows:<br>Note 1:<br>For aggregations that do not allow all of the CEs to run simultaneously, the possible combination of CEs that provides the largest CTP should be used. The TP of each contributing CE is to be calculated at its maximum value theoretically possible before the CTP of the combination is derived.<br>N.B. To determine the possible combinations of simultaneously operating CEs, generate an instruction sequence that initiates operations in multiple CEs, beginning with the slowest CE (the one needing the largest number of cycles to complete its operation) and ending with the fastest CE. At each cycle of the sequence, the combination of CEs that are in operation during that cycle is a possible combination. The instruction sequence must take into account all hardware and/or architectural constraints on overlapping operations.<br>Note 2:<br>A single integrated circuit chip or board assembly may contain multiple CEs.<br>Note 3:<br>Simultaneous operations are assumed to exist when the computer manufacturer claims concurrent, parallel or simultaneous operation or execution in a manual or brochure for the computer.<br>Note 4:<br>CTP values are not to be aggregated for CE combinations (inter) connected by local area networks, Wide Area Networks, Input/Output shared connections/devices, Input/Output controllers and any communication interconnection implemented by software.<br>Note 5:<br>CTP values must be aggregated for multiple CEs specially designed to enhance performance by aggregation, operating simultaneously and sharing memory,—or multiple memory/CE—combinations operating simultaneously utilising specially designed hardware.<br>This aggregation does not apply to assemblies described by entry 4A003d<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>where<br>the TPs are ordered by value, with TP<sub>1</sub> being the highest, TP<sub>2</sub> being the second highest,…, and TP<sub>n</sub> being the lowest. C<sub>i</sub> is a coefficient determined by the strength of the interconnection between CEs, as follows:<br>For multiple CEs operating simultaneously and sharing memory:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>Note 1:<br>When the CTP calculated by the above method does not exceed 194 Mtops, the following formula may be used to calculate C<sub>i</sub>:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>where<br>m<br>number of CEs or groups of CEs sharing access.<br>provided:<br>1. The TPi of each CE or group of CEs does not exceed 30 Mtops;<br>2. The CEs or groups of CEs share access to main memory (excluding cache memory) over a single channel; and<br>3. Only one CE or group of CEs can have use of the channel at any given time.<br>N.B. This does not apply to items controlled under Category 3.<br>Note 2:<br>CEs share memory if they access a common segment of solid state memory. This memory may include cache memory, main memory, or other internal memory. Peripheral memory devices such as disk drives, tape drives or RAM disks are not included.<br>For Multiple CEs or groups of CEs not sharing memory, interconnected by one or more data channels:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>The value of Ci is based on the number of CEs, not the number of nodes.<br>where<br>k<sub>i</sub><br>min (S<sub>i</sub>/K<sub>r</sub>, 1), and<br>K<sub>r</sub><br>normalizing factor of 20 MByte/s<br>S<sub>i</sub><br>sum of the maximum data rates (in units of MByte/s) for all data channels connected to the i<sup>hh</sup> CE or group of CEs sharing memory<br>When calculating a C<sub>i</sub> for a group of CEs, the number of the first CE in a group determines the proper limits for C<sub>i</sub>. For example, in an aggregation of groups consisting of 3 CEs each, the 22<sup>nd</sup> group will contain CE<sub>64</sub>, CE<sub>65</sub> and CE<sub>66</sub>. The proper limit for C<sub>i</sub> for this group is 0.60.<br>Aggregation (of CEs or groups of CEs) should be from fastest-to-slowest; i.e.:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>, and<br>in the case of TP<sub>i</sub> = TPsubi;+<sub>1</sub> from the largest to smallest; i.e.:<br><a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1995/271">formula: see source</a><br>Note:<br>The k<sub>i</sub> factor is not applied to CEs 2 to 12 if the TP<sub>i</sub> of the CE or group of CEs is more than 50 Mtops; i.e., C<sub>i</sub> for CEs 2 to 12 is 0.75.</td></tr>
<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 5— TELECOMMUNICATIONS AND “INFORMATION SECURITY”</th></tr>
<tr><th>Part 1—TELECOMMUNICATIONS</th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2">NOTES:<br>1. The control status of components, “lasers”, test and production equipment, materials and “software” therefor, which are specially designed for telecommunications equipment or systems is defined in this Category.<br>2. “Digital computers”, related equipment or “software”, when essential for the operation and support of telecommunications equipment described in this Category, are regarded as specially designed components, provided they are the standard models customarily supplied by the manufacturer. This includes operation, administration, maintenance, engineering or billing computer systems.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5A1</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5A001</strong></td><td>a. Any type of telecommunications equipment having any of the following characteristics, functions or features:<br>1. Specially designed to withstand transitory electronic effects or electromagnetic pulse arising from a nuclear explosion;<br>2. Specially hardened to withstand gamma, neutron or ion radiation;<br>3. Specially designed to operate outside the temperature range from 218 K (−55° C) to 397 K (124° C);<br>NOTE: 5A001.a.3. applies only to electronic equipment.<br>NOTE: 5A001.a.2. and 3. do not apply to equipment on board satellites.<br>b. Telecommunication transmission equipment or systems, and specially designed components and accessories therefor, having any of the following characteristics, functions or features:<br>NOTE: Telecommunication transmission equipment:<br>a. Categorised as follows, or combinations thereof:<br>1. Radio equipment (e.g., transmitters, receivers and transceivers);<br>2. Line terminating equipment;<br>3. Intermediate amplifier equipment;<br>4. Repeater equipment;<br>5. Regenerator equipment;<br>6. Translation encoders (transcoders);<br>7. Multiplex equipment (statistical multiplex included);<br>8. Modulators/demodulators (modems);<br>9. Transmultiplex equipment (see CCITT Rec. G.701);<br>10. “Stored programme controlled” digital crossconnection equipment;<br>11. “Gateways” and bridges;<br>12. “Media access units”; and<br>b. Designed for use in single or multi-channel communication via:<br>1. Wire (line);<br>2. Coaxial cable;<br>3. Optical fibre cable;<br>4. Electromagnetic radiation;<br>5. Underwater acoustic wave propagation.<br>1. Employing digital techniques, including digital processing of analogue signals, and designed to operate at a “digital transfer rate” at the highest multiplex level exceeding 45 Mbit/s or a “total digital transfer rate” exceeding 90 Mbit/s;<br>NOTE: 5A001.b.1. does not control equipment specially designed to be integrated and operated in any satellite system for civil use.<br>2. Being “stored programme controlled” digital cross connect equipment with a “digital transfer rate” exceeding 8.5 Mbit/s per port;<br>3. Being equipment containing:<br>a. Modems using the “bandwidth of one voice channel” with a “data signalling rate” exceeding 28,800 bits/s;<br>b. “Communication channel controllers” with a digital output having a “data signalling rate” exceeding 2.1 Mbit/s per channel; or<br>c. “Network access controllers” and their related common medium having a “digital transfer rate” exceeding 156 Mbit/s;<br>NOTE: If any non-controlled equipment contains a “network access controller”, it cannot have any type of telecommunications interface;<br>except:<br>Those described in, but not controlled by, 5A001.b.3.<br>4. Employing a “laser” and having any of the following characteristics:<br>a. A transmission wavelength exceeding 1000 nm;<br>b. Employing analogue techniques and having a bandwidth exceeding 45 MHz;<br>c. Employing coherent optical transmission or coherent optical detection techniques (also called optical heterodyne or homodyne techniques);<br>d. Employing wavelength division multiplexing techniques; or<br>e. Performing “optical amplification”;<br>5. Being radio equipment operating at input or output frequencies exceeding:<br>a. 31 GHz for satellite-earth station applications;<br>b. 26.5 GHz for other applications;<br>NOTE: 5A001.b.5.b. does not specify equipment for civil use conforming with an International Telecommunications Union (ITU) allocated band between 26.5 and 31 GHz.<br>6. Being radio equipment:<br>a. Employing quadrature-amplitude-modulation (QAM) techniques above level 4 if the “total digital transfer rate” exceeds 8.5 Mbit/s;<br>b. Employing quadrature-amplitude-modulation (QAM) techniques above level 16 if the “total digital transfer rate” is equal to or less than 8.5 Mbit/s; or<br>c. Employing other digital modulation techniques and having a “spectral efficiency” exceeding 3 bit/sec/Hz;<br>NOTE:<br>1. 5A001.b.6. does not specify equipment specially designed to be integrated and operated in any satellite system for civil use.<br>2. 5A001.b.6. does not specify radio relay equipment for operation in an International Telecommunications Union (ITU) allocated band:<br>a. 1. Not exceeding 960 Mhz; or<br>2. With a “total digital transfer rate” not exceeding 8.5 Mbits/s; and<br>b. Having a “spectral efficiency” not exceeding 4 bits/sec/Hz.<br>7. Being radio equipment operating in the 1.5 to 87.5 MHz band and having either of the following characteristics:<br>a. 1. Automatically predicting and selecting frequencies and “total digital transfer rates” per channel to optimize the transmission; and<br>2. Incorporating a linear power amplifier configuration having a capability to support multiple signals simultaneously at an output power of 1 kW or more in the 1.5 to 30 MHz frequency range or 250 W or more in the 30 to 87.5 MHz frequency range, over an “instantaneous bandwidth” of one octave or more and with an output harmonic and distortion content of better than —80 dB; or<br>b. Incorporating adaptive techniques providing more than 15 dB suppression of an interfering signal;<br>8. Being radio equipment employing “spread spectrum” or “frequency agility” (frequency hopping) techniques having either of the following characteristics:<br>a. User programmable spreading codes; or<br>b. A total transmitted bandwidth which is 100 or more times the bandwidth of any one information channel and in excess of 50 kHz;<br>9. Being digitally controlled radio receivers having more than 1,000 channels, which:<br>a. Search or scan automatically a part of the electromagnetic spectrum;<br>b. Identify the received signals or the type of transmitter; and<br>c. Have a “frequency switching time” of less than 1 ms;<br>10. Providing functions of digital “signal processing” as follows:<br>a. Voice coding at rates of less than 2,400 bit/s;<br>b. Employing circuitry which incorporates “user-accessible programmability” of digital “signal processing” circuits exceeding the limits of 4A003.b.;<br>11. Being underwater communications systems having any of the following characteristics:<br>a. An acoustic carrier frequency outside the range from 20 to 60 kHz;<br>b. Using an electromagnetic carrier frequency below 30 kHz; or<br>c. Using electronic beam steering techniques;<br>c. “Stored programme controlled” switching equipment and related signalling systems, having any of the following characteristics, functions or features, and specially designed components and accessories therefor:<br>NOTE: Statistical multiplexers with digital input and digital output which provide switching are treated as “stored programme controlled” switches.<br>1. “Common channel signalling”;<br>NOTE: Signalling systems in which the signalling channel is carried in and refers to no more than 32 multiplexed channels forming a trunk line of no more than 2.1 Mbit/s, and in which the signalling information is carried in a fixed, time division multiplexed channel without the use of labelled messages, are not considered to be “common channel signalling” systems.<br>2. Containing “Integrated Services Digital Network” (ISDN) functions and having either of the following:<br>a. Switch-terminal (e.g., subscriber line) interfaces with a “digital transfer rate” at the highest multiplex level exceeding 192,000 bit/s, including the associated signalling channel (e.g., 2B+D); or<br>b. The capability that a signalling message received by a switch on a given channel that is related to a communication on another channel may be passed through to another switch;<br>NOTE: 5A001.c.2. does not preclude:<br>1. The evaluation and appropriate actions taken by the receiving switch;<br>2. Unrelated user message traffic on a D channel of “ISDN”.<br>3. Multi-level priority and pre-emption for circuit switching;<br>NOTE: 5A001.c.3. does not specify single-level call pre-emption.<br>4. “Dynamic adaptive routing”;<br>5. Routing or switching of “datagram” packets;<br>6. Routing or switching of “fast select” packets;<br>NOTE: The restrictions in 5A001.c.5. and 6. do not apply to networks using only “network access controllers” or to “network access controllers” themselves.<br>7. Designed for automatic hand-off of cellular radio calls to other cellular switches or for automatic connection to a centralized subscriber data base common to more than one switch;<br>8. Being packet switches, circuit switches and routers with ports or lines exceeding either:<br>a. A “data signalling rate” of 64,000 bit/s per channel for a “communications channel controller”; or<br>NOTE: 5A001.c.8.a. does not preclude the multiplexing over a composite link of communications channels not specified in 5A001.c.8.a.<br>b. A “digital transfer rate” of 33 Mbit/s for a “network access controller” and related common medium;<br>9. “Optical switching”;<br>10. Employing “Asynchronous Transfer Mode” (ATM) techniques;<br>11. Containing “stored programme controlled” digital crossconnect equipment with a “digital transfer rate” exceeding 8.5 Mbit/s per port;<br>d. Centralized network control having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Receives data from the nodes; and<br>2. Processes these data in order to provide control of traffic not requiring operator decisions, thereby performing “dynamic adaptive routing”;<br>NOTE: 5A001.d. does not preclude control of traffic as a function of predictable statistical traffic conditions.<br>e. Optical fibre communication cables, optical fibres and accessories, as follows:<br>1. Optical fibres or cables of more than 50 m in length having either of the following characteristics:<br>a. Designed for single mode operation; or<br>b. For optical fibres, specified by the manufacturer as being capable of withstanding a Proof Test tensile stress of 2 × 10<sup>9</sup> N/m<sup>2</sup> or more;<br>Technical Note: Proof Test: On-line or off-line production screen testing that dynamically applies a prescribed tensile stress over a 0.5 to 3 m length of fibre at a running rate of 2 to 5 m/s while passing between capstans approximately 150 mm in diameter. The ambient temperature is a nominal 293 K (20° C) and relative humidity of 40%.<br>N.B.: Equivalent national standards may be used for executing the Proof Test.<br>2. Optical fibre cables and accessories designed for underwater use (for fibre-optic hull penetrators or connectors, see 8A002.c.);<br>f. Phased array antennae, operating above 10.5 GHz, containing active elements and distributed components, and designed to permit electronic control of beam shaping and pointing.<br>NOTE: This sub-item does not specify landing systems with instruments meeting International Civil Aviation Organisation (ICAO) standards (microwave landing systems (MLS)).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5A101</strong></td><td>Telemetering and telecontrol equipment usable for “missiles”.<br>NOTE: This item does not control equipment specially designed to be used for remote control of model planes, boats or vehicles and having an electric field strength of not more than 200 microvolts per metre at a distance of 500 metres.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5B1</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5B001</strong></td><td>a. Equipment, and specially designed components and accessories therefor, specially designed for:<br>1. Development of equipment, materials, functions or features specified in 5A001, 5B001, 5C001, 5D001 or 5E001, including measuring or test equipment;<br>2. Production of equipment, materials, functions or features specified in 5A001, 5B001, 5C001, 5D001 or 5E001, including measuring, test or repair equipment;<br>3. Use of equipment, materials, functions or features exceeding any of the least stringent control criteria applicable in 5A001, 5B001, 5C001, 5D001 or 5E001, including measuring, repair or test equipment;<br>NOTE: 5B001.a. does not control optical fibres and “optical fibre preform” characterisation equipment not using semiconductor “lasers”.<br>b. Other equipment as follows:<br>1. Bit error rate (BER) test equipment designed or modified to test the equipment specified in 5A001.b.1.;<br>2. Data communication protocol analyzers, testers and simulators specially designed for functions specified in 5A001;<br>3. Stand alone “stored programme controlled” radio transmission media simulators/channel estimators specially designed for testing equipment specified in 5A001.b.5.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5C1</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5C001</strong></td><td>Preforms of glass or of any other material optimized for the manufacture of optical fibres specified in 5A001.e.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5D1</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5D001</strong></td><td>a. “Software” specially designed or modified for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment or materials specified in 5A001, 5B001 or 5C001;<br>b. “Software” specially designed or modified to support “technology” specified in 5E001;<br>c. Specific “software” as follows:<br>1. “Generic software”, other than in machine-executable form, specially designed or modified for the “use” of “stored programme controlled” digital switching equipment or systems;<br>2. “Software”, other than in machine-executable form, specially designed or modified for the “use” of digital cellular radio equipment or systems;<br>3. “Software” specially designed or modified to provide characteristics, functions or features of equipment specified in 5A001 or 5B001;<br>4. “Software” which provides the capability of recovering “source code” of telecommunications “software” specified in this Category;<br>5. “Software” specially designed for the “development” or “production” of “software” specified in 5D001.<br>(For “software” for “signal processing” see also 4D. and 6D.)</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5E1</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5E001</strong></td><td>a. “Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development”, “production” or “use” (excluding operation) of equipment, systems, materials or “software” specified in 5A001, 5B001, 5C001 or 5D001;<br>b. Specific technologies, as follows:<br>1. “Required” “technology” for the “development” or “production” of telecommunications equipment specially designed to be used on board satellites;<br>2. “Technology” for the “development” or “use” of laser communication techniques with the capability of automatically acquiring and tracking signals and maintaining communications through exoatmosphere or sub-surface (water) media;<br>3. “Technology” for the processing and application of coatings to optical fibre specially designed to make it suitable for underwater use;<br>4. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of equipment employing “Synchronous Digital Hierarchy” (SDH) or “Synchronous Optical Network” (SONET) techniques;<br>5. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of “switch fabric” exceeding 64,000 bit/s per information channel other than for digital cross connect integrated in the switch;<br>6. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of centralized network control;<br>7. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of digital cellular radio systems;<br>8. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of “Integrated Services Digital Network” (ISDN);<br>9. “Technology” for the “development” of QAM techniques, for radio equipment, above level 4.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5E101</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment specified in 5A101.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2">Part 2—“INFORMATION SECURITY”</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>NOTE: The control status of “information security” equipment, “software”, systems, application specific “electronic assemblies”, modules, integrated circuits, components or functions is defined in this Category even if they are components or “electronic assemblies” of other equipment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5A2</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5A002</strong></td><td>Systems, equipment, application specific “electronic assemblies”, modules or integrated circuits for “information security”, as follows, and other specially designed components therefor:<br>a. Designed or modified to use “cryptography” employing digital techniques to ensure “information security”;<br>b. Designed or modified to perform cryptanalytic functions;<br>c. Designed or modified to use “cryptography” employing analogue techniques to ensure “information security”;<br>except:<br>1. Equipment using “fixed” band scrambling not exceeding 8 bands and in which the transpositions change not more frequently than once every second;<br>2. Equipment using “fixed” band scrambling exceeding 8 bands and in which the transpositions change not more frequently than once every ten seconds;<br>3. Equipment using “fixed” frequency inversion and in which the transpositions change not more frequently than once every second;<br>4. Facsimile equipment;<br>5. Restricted audience broadcast equipment;<br>6. Civil television equipment;<br>d. Designed or modified to suppress the compromising emanations of information-bearing signals;<br>NOTE: 5A002.d. does not specify equipment specially designed to suppress emanations for health or safety reasons.<br>e. Designed or modified to use cryptographic techniques to generate the spreading code for “spread spectrum” or the hopping code for “frequency agility” systems;<br>f. Designed or modified to provide certified or certifiable “multilevel security” or user isolation at a level exceeding Class B2 of the Trusted Computer System Evaluation Criteria (TCSEC) or equivalent;<br>g. Communications cable systems designed or modified using mechanical, electrical or electronic means to detect surreptitious intrusion.<br>NOTE: 5A002 does not control:<br>a. “Personalized smart cards” using “cryptography” restricted for use only in equipment or systems, as follows:<br>1. Excluded from control under 5A002.c.1. to c.6.;<br>2. Excluded from control under b., c. or e. of this Note;<br>3. a. Access control equipment, such as automatic teller machines, self-service statement printers or point of sale terminals, which protects password or personal identification numbers (PIN) or similar data to prevent unauthorised access to facilities but does not allow for encryption of files or text, except as directly related to the password or PIN protection;<br>b. Data authentication equipment which calculates a Message Authentication Code (MAC) or similar result to ensure no alteration of text has taken place, or to authenticate users, but does not allow for encryption of data, text or other media other than that needed for the authentication;<br>c. Cryptographic equipment specially designed, developed or modified for use in machines for banking or money transactions, such as automatic teller machines, self-service statement printers, point of sale terminals, or equipment for the encryption of interbanking transactions, and intended for use only in such applications;<br>d. Portable (personal) or mobile radiotelephones for civil use, e.g., for use with commercial civil cellular radiocommunications systems, containing encryption;<br>b. Equipment containing “fixed” data compression or coding techniques;<br>c. Receiving equipment for radio broadcast, pay television or similar restricted audience television of the consumer type, without digital encryption and where digital decryption is limited to the video, audio or management functions;<br>d. Portable (personal) or mobile radiotelephones for civil use, e.g., for use with commercial civil cellular radiocommunications systems, containing encryption, when accompanying their users;<br>e. Decryption functions specially designed to allow the execution of copy-protected “software”, provided the decryption functions are not user-accessible.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5B2</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5B002</strong></td><td>a. Equipment specially designed for:<br>1. The development of equipment or functions specified in 5A002, 5B002, 5D002 or 5E002, including measuring or test equipment;<br>2. The production of equipment or functions specified in 5A002, 5B002, 5D002 or 5E002, including measuring, test, repair or production equipment;<br>b. Measuring equipment specially designed to evaluate and validate the “information security” functions specified in 5A002 or 5D002.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5C2</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>NONE</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5D2</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5D002</strong></td><td>a. “Software” specially designed or modified for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment or “software” specified in 5A002, 5B002 or 5D002;<br>b. “Software” specially designed or modified to support “technology” specified in 5E002;<br>c. Specific “software” as follows:<br>1. “Software” having the characteristics, or performing or simulating the functions of the equipment specified in 5A002 or 5B002;<br>2. “Software” to certify “software” specified in 5D002.c.1.;<br>3. “Software” designed or modified to protect against malicious computer damage, e.g., viruses;<br>NOTE: 5D002 does not control:<br>a. “Software” “required” for the “use” of equipment excluded from control under the Note to 5A002;<br>b. “Software” providing any of the functions of equipment excluded from control under the Note to 5A002.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5E2</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>5E002</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment or “software” specified in 5A002, 5B002 or 5D002.</td></tr>
<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 6— SENSORS AND LASERS</th></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A001</strong></td><td><strong>Acoustics</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a. Marine acoustic systems, equipment or specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>1. Active (transmitting or transmitting-and-receiving) systems, equipment or specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>NOTE: 6A001.a.1. does not control:<br>a. Depth sounders operating vertically below the apparatus, not including a scanning function exceeding ± 10°, and limited to measuring the depth of water, the distance of submerged or buried objects or fish finding;<br>b. Acoustic beacons, as follows:<br>1. Acoustic emergency beacons;<br>2. Pingers specially designed for relocating or returning to an underwater position.<br>a. Wide-swath bathymetric survey systems for sea bed topographic mapping:<br>1. Designed:<br>a. To take measurements at an angle exceeding 10° from the vertical; and<br>b. To measure depths exceeding 600 m below the water surface; and<br>2. Designed:<br>a. To incorporate multiple beams any of which is less than 2°; or<br>b. To provide data accuracies of better than 0.5% of water depth across the swath averaged over the individual measurements within the swath;<br>b. Object detection or location systems having any of the following:<br>1. A transmitting frequency below 10 kHz;<br>2. Sound pressure level exceeding 224 dB (reference 1 micropascal at 1 m) for equipment with an operating frequency in the band from 10 kHz to 24 kHz inclusive;<br>3. Sound pressure level exceeding 235 dB (reference 1 micropascal at 1 m) for equipment with an operating frequency in the band between 24 kHz and 30 kHz;<br>4. Forming beams of less than 1° on any axis and having an operating frequency of less than 100 kHz;<br>5. Designed to withstand pressure during normal operation at depths exceeding 1,000 m and having transducers:<br>a. Dynamically compensated for pressure; or<br>b. Incorporating other than lead zirconate titanate as the transduction element; or<br>6. Designed to operate with an unambiguous display range exceeding 5,120 m;<br>c. Acoustic projectors, including transducers, incorporating piezoelectric, magnetostrictive, electrostrictive, electrodynamic or hydraulic elements operating individually or in a designed combination, having any of the following:<br>NOTES:<br>1. The control status of acoustic projectors, including transducers, specially designed for other equipment is determined by the control status of the other equipment.<br>2. 6A001.a.1.c. does not control sources which direct the sound vertically only, or mechanical (e.g., air gun or vapour-shock gun) or chemical (e.g., explosives) sources.<br>1. An instantaneous radiated acoustic power density exceeding 0.01 mW/mm<sup>2</sup>/Hz for devices operating at frequencies below 10 kHz;<br>2. A continuously radiated acoustic power density exceeding 0.001 mW/mm<sup>2</sup>/Hz for devices operating at frequencies below 10 kHz;<br>Technical Note: Acoustic power density is obtained by dividing the output acoustic power by the product of the area of the radiating surface and the frequency of operation.<br>3. Designed to withstand pressure during normal operation at depths exceeding 1,000 m; or<br>4. Side-lobe suppression exceeding 22 dB;<br>d. Acoustic systems, equipment and specially designed components for determining the position of surface vessels or underwater vehicles designed:<br>NOTES: 6A001.a.1.d. includes equipment using coherent “signal processing” between two or more beacons and the hydrophone unit carried by the surface vessel or underwater vehicle, or capable of automatically correcting speed-of-sound propagation errors for calculation of a point.<br>1. To operate at a range exceeding 1,000 m with a positioning accuracy of less than 10 m rms (root mean square) when measured at a range of 1,000 m; or<br>2. To withstand pressure at depths exceeding 1,000 m;<br>2. Passive (receiving, whether or not related in normal application to separate active equipment) systems, equipment or specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>a. Hydrophones (transducers) with any of the following characteristics:<br>1. Incorporating continuous flexible sensors or assemblies of discrete sensor elements with either a diameter or length less than 20 mm and with a separation between elements of less than 20 mm;<br>2. Having any of the following sensing elements:<br>a. Optical fibres;<br>b. Piezoelectric polymers; or<br>c. Flexible piezoelectric ceramic materials;<br>3. Hydrophone sensitivity better than 180 dB at any depth with no acceleration compensation;<br>4. When designed to operate at depths not exceeding 35 m, hydrophone sensitivity better than −186 dB with acceleration compensation;<br>5. When designed for normal operation at depths exceeding 35 m, hydrophone sensitivity better than −192 dB with acceleration compensation;<br>6. When designed for normal operation at depths exceeding 100 m, hydrophone sensitivity better than −204 dB; or<br>7. Designed for operation at depths exceeding 1,000 m;<br>Technical Note: Hydrophone sensitivity is defined as twenty times the logarithm to the base 10 of the ratio of rms output voltage to a 1 V rms reference, when the hydrophone sensor, without a pre-amplifier, is placed in a plane wave acoustic field with an rms pressure of 1 micropascal. For example, a hydrophone of −160 dB (reference 1 V per micropascal) would yield an output voltage of 10<sup>−8</sup> V in such a field, while one of −180 dB sensitivity would yield only 10<sup>−9</sup> V output. Thus, −160 dB is better than −180 dB.<br>b. Towed acoustic hydrophone arrays with any of the following:<br>1. Hydrophone group spacing of less than 12.5 m;<br>2. Hydrophone group spacing of 12.5 m to less than 25 m and designed or able to be modified to operate at depths exceeding 35 m; or<br>Technical Note: ‘Able to be modified’ in 6A001.a.2.b.2. means having provisions to allow a change of the wiring or interconnections to alter hydrophone group spacing or operating depth limits. These provisions are: spare wiring exceeding 10% of the number of wires, hydrophone group spacing adjustment blocks or internal depth limiting devices that are adjustable or that control more than one hydrophone group.<br>3. Hydrophone group spacing of 25 m or more and designed to operate at depths exceeding 100 m;<br>4. Heading sensors controlled by 6A001.a.2.d.;<br>5. Non-metallic strength members or longitudinally reinforced array hoses;<br>6. An assembled array of less than 40 mm in diameter;<br>7. Multiplexed hydrophone group signals; or<br>8. Hydrophone characteristics specified in 6A001.a.2.a.;<br>c. Processing equipment, specially designed for towed acoustic hydrophone arrays, with either of the following:<br>1. A Fast Fourier or other transform of 1,024 or more complex points in less than 20 ms with no “user-accessible programmability”; or<br>2. Time or frequency domain processing and correlation, including spectral analysis, digital filtering and beamforming using Fast Fourier or other transforms or processes with “user-accessible programmability”;<br>d. Heading sensors having an accuracy of better than ±0.5°; and<br>1. Designed to be incorporated within the array hosing and to operate at depths exceeding 35 m or having an adjustable or removable depth sensing device in order to operate at depths exceeding 35m; or<br>2. Designed to be mounted external to the array hosing and having a sensor unit capable of operating with 360° roll at depths exceeding 35 m;<br>b. Terrestrial geophones capable of conversion for use in marine systems, equipment or specially designed components specified in 6A001.a.2.a.;<br>c. Correlation-velocity sonar log equipment designed to measure the horizontal speed of the equipment carrier relative to the sea bed at distances between the carrier and the sea bed exceeding 500 m.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A002</strong></td><td><strong>Optical sensors</strong><br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 6A102.<br>a. Optical detectors, as follows:<br>NOTE: 6A002.a. does not control germanium or silicon photodevices.<br>1. “Space-qualified” solid-state detectors having any of the following:<br>a. 1. A peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 10 nm but not exceeding 300 nm; and<br>2. A response of less than 0.1% relative to the peak response at a wavelength exceeding 400 nm;<br>b. 1. A peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 900 nm but not exceeding 1,200 nm; and<br>2. A response “time constant” of 95 ns or less; or<br>c. A peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 1,200 nm but not exceeding 30,000 nm;<br>2. Image intensifier tubes and specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>a. Image intensifier tubes having all of the following:<br>1. A peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 400 nm but not exceeding 1,050 nm;<br>2. A microchannel plate for electron image amplification with a hole pitch (centre-to-centre spacing) of less than 25 micrometres; and<br>3. a. An S-20, S-25 or multialkali photocathode; or<br>b. A GaAs or GaInAs photocathode;<br>b. Specially designed components, as follows:<br>1. Fibre optic image inverters;<br>2. Microchannel plates having both of the following characteristics:<br>a. 1,5000 or more hollow tubes per plate; and<br>b. Hole pitch (centre-to-centre spacing) of less than 25 micrometres;<br>3. GaAs or GaInAs photocathodes;<br>3. Non-“space-qualified” “focal plane arrays”, having any of the following:<br>Technical Note: Linear or two-dimensional multi-element detector arrays are referred to as “focal plane arrays”.<br>NOTES:<br>1. 6A002.a.3. includes photoconductive arrays and photovoltaic arrays.<br>2. 6A002.a.3. does not specify silicon “focal plane arrays”, multi-element (not to exceed 16 elements) encapsulated photoconductive cells or pyroelectric detectors using any of the following:<br>a. Lead sulphide;<br>2 b. Triglycine sulphate and variants;<br>c. Lead-lanthanum-zirconium titanate and variants;<br>d. Lithium tantalate;<br>e. Polyvinylidene fluoride and variants;<br>f. Strontium barium niobate and variants; or<br>g. Lead selenide.<br>a. 1. Individual elements with a peak response within the wavelength range exceeding 900 nm but not exceeding 1,050 nm; and<br>2. A response “time constant” of less than 0.5 ns;<br>b. 1. Individual elements with a peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 1,050 nm but not exceeding 1,200 nm; and<br>2. A response “time constant” of 95 ns or less; or<br>c. Individual elements with a peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 1,200 nm but not exceeding 30,000 nm;<br>4. Non-“space-qualified” single-element or non-focal-plane multi-element semiconductor photodiodes or phototransistors having both of the following:<br>a. A peak response in the wavelength range exceeding 1,200 nm but not exceeding 30,000 nm; and<br>b. A response “time constant” of 0.5 ns or less;<br>b. “Multispectral imaging sensors” designed for remote sensing applications, having either of the following characteristics:<br>1. An Instantaneous-Field-Of-View (IFOV) of less than 200 microradians; or<br>2. Specified for operation in the wavelength range exceeding 400 nm but not exceeding 30,000 nm; and<br>a. Providing output imaging data in digital format; and<br>b. 1. “Space-qualified”; or<br>2. Designed for airborne operation, using other than silicon detectors, and having an IFOV of less than 2.5 milliradians;<br>c. Direct view imaging equipment operating in the visible or infrared spectrum, incorporating either of the following:<br>1. Image intensifier tubes specified in 6A002.a.2.; or<br>2. “Focal plane arrays” specified in 6A002.a.3.;<br>Technical Note: ‘Direct view’ refers to imaging equipment, operating in the visible or infrared spectrum, that presents a visual image to a human observer without converting the image into an electronic signal for television display, and that cannot record or store the image photographically, electronically or by any other means.<br>NOTE: 6A002.c. does not specify the following equipment incorporating other than GaAs or GaInAs photocathodes:<br>a. Industrial or civilian intrusion alarm, traffic or industrial movement control or counting systems;<br>b. Medical equipment;<br>c. Industrial equipment used for inspection, sorting or analysis of the properties of materials;<br>d. Flame detectors for industrial furnaces;<br>e. Equipment specially designed for laboratory use.<br>d. Special support components for optical sensors, as follows:<br>1. “Space-qualified” cryocoolers;<br>2. Non-“space-qualified” cryocoolers with a cooling source temperature below 218 K (−55°C), as follows:<br>a. Closed cycle with a specified Mean-Time-To-Failure (MTTF), or Mean-Time-Between-Failures (MTBF), exceeding 2,500 hours;<br>b. Joule-Thomson (JT) self-regulating minicoolers with bore (outside) diameters of less than 8 mm;<br>3. Optical sensing fibres:<br>a. Specially fabricated either compositionally or structurally, or modified by coating, to be acoustically, thermally, inertially, electromagnetically or nuclear radiation sensitive; or<br>b. Modified structurally to have a “beat length” of less than 50 mm (high birefringence).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A003</strong></td><td><strong>Cameras</strong><br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 6A203.<br>a. Instrumentation cameras, as follows:<br>1. High-speed cinema recording cameras using any film format from 8 mm to 16 mm inclusive, in which the film is continuously advanced throughout the recording period, and that are capable of recording at framing rates exceeding 13,150 frames per second;<br>NOTE: 6A003.a.1. does not control cinema recording cameras for normal civil purposes.<br>2. Mechanical high speed cameras, in which the film does not move, capable of recording at rates exceeding 1,000,000 frames per second for the full framing height of 35 mm film, or at proportionately higher rates for lesser frame heights, or at proportionately lower rates for greater frame heights;<br>3. Mechanical or electronic streak cameras with writing speeds exceeding 10 mm per microsecond;<br>4. Electronic framing cameras having a speed exceeding 1,000,000 frames per second;<br>5. Electronic cameras having:<br>a. An electronic shutter speed (gating capability) of less than 1 microsecond per full frame; and<br>b. A read out time allowing a framing rate of more than 125 full frames per second;<br>b. Imaging cameras, as follows:<br>NOTE: 6A003.b. does not control television or video cameras specially designed for television broadcasting.<br>1. Video cameras incorporating solid state sensors, having any of the following:<br>a. More than 4 × 10<sup>6</sup> “active pixels” per solid state array for monochrome (black and white) cameras;<br>b. More than 4 × 10<sup>6</sup> “active pixels” per solid state array for colour cameras incorporating three solid state arrays; or<br>c. More than 12 × 10<sup>6</sup> “active pixels” for solid state array colour cameras incorporating one solid state array;<br>2. Scanning cameras and scanning camera systems:<br>a. Incorporating linear detector arrays with more than 8,192 elements per array; and<br>b. Having mechanical scanning in one direction;<br>3. Incorporating image intensifiers specified in 6A002.a.2.a.;<br>4. Incorporating focal plane arrays specified in 6A002.a.3.;<br>N.B.: For cameras specially designed or modified for underwater use, see 8A002.d. and 8A002.e.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A004</strong></td><td><strong>Optics</strong><br>a. Optical mirrors (reflectors), as follows:<br>1. “Deformable mirrors” with either continuous or multi-element surfaces, and specially designed components therefor, capable of dynamically repositioning portions of the surface of the mirror at rates exceeding 100 Hz;<br>2. Lightweight monolithic mirrors with an average “equivalent density” of less than 30 kg/m<sup>2</sup> and a total weight exceeding 10 kg;<br>3. Lightweight “composite” or foam mirror structures with an average “equivalent density” of less than 30 kg/m<sup>2</sup> and a total weight exceeding 2 kg;<br>4. Beam steering mirrors more than 100 mm in diameter or length of major axis which maintain a flatness of lambda/2 or better (lambda is equal to 633 nm) with a control bandwidth exceeding 100 Hz;<br>b. Optical components made from zinc selenide (ZnSe) or zinc sulphide (ZnS) with transmission in the wavelength range exceeding 3,000 nm but not exceeding 25,000 nm and either of the following:<br>1. Exceeding 100cm<sup>3</sup> in volume; or<br>2. Exceeding 80 mm in diameter or length of major axis and 20 mm in thickness (depth);<br>c. “Space-qualified” components for optical systems, as follows:<br>1. Lightweighted to less than 20% “equivalent density” compared with a solid blank of the same aperture and thickness;<br>2. Substrates, substrates with surface coatings (single-layer or multi-layer, metallic or dielectric, conducting, semiconducting or insulating) or with protective films;<br>3. Segments or assemblies of mirrors designed to be assembled in space into an optical system with a collecting aperture equivalent to or larger than a single optic 1 metre in diameter;<br>4. Manufactured from “composite” materials having a coefficient of linear thermal expansion equal to or less than 5 × 10<sup>−6</sup> in any coordinate direction;<br>d. Optical filters, as follows:<br>1. For wavelengths longer than 250 nm, comprised of multi-layer optical coatings and having either of the following:<br>a. Bandwidths equal to or less than 1 nm Full Width Half Intensity (FWHI) and peak transmission of 90% or more; or<br>b. Bandwidths equal to or less than 0.1 nm FWHI and peak transmission of 50% or more;<br>NOTE: 6A004.d.1. does not control optical filters with fixed air gaps or Lyot-type filters.<br>2. For wavelengths longer than 250 nm, having all of the following:<br>a. Tunable over a spectral range of 500 nm or more;<br>b. Instantaneous optical bandpass of 1.25 nm or less;<br>c. Wavelength resettable within 0.1 ms to an accuracy of 1 nm or better within the tunable spectral range; and<br>d. A single peak transmission of 91% or more;<br>3. Optical opacity switches (filters) with a field of view of 30° or wider and a response time equal to or less than 1 ns;<br>e. Optical control equipment, as follows:<br>1. Specially designed to maintain the surface figure or orientation of the “space-qualified” components specified in 6A004.c.1. or 3.;<br>2. Having steering, tracking, stabilization or resonator alignment bandwidths equal to or more than 100 Hz and an accuracy of 10 microradians or less;<br>3. Gimbals having a maximum slew exceeding 5°, a bandwidth equal to or more than 100 Hz, and either of the following:<br>a. 1. Exceeding 0.15 m but not exceeding 1 m in diameter or major axis length;<br>2. Capable of angular accelerations exceeding 2 radians/s<sup>2</sup>; and<br>3. Having angular pointing errors equal to or less than 200 microradians; or<br>b. 1. Exceeding 1 m in diameter or major axis length;<br>2. Capable of angular accelerations exceeding 0.5 radians/s<sup>2</sup>; and<br>3. Having angular pointing errors equal to or less than 200 microradians;<br>4. Specially designed to maintain the alignment of phased array or phased segment mirror systems consisting of mirrors with a segment diameter or major axis length of 1 m or more;<br>f. “Fluoride fibre” cable, or optical fibres therefor, having an attenuation of less than 4 dB/km in the wavelength range exceeding 1,000 nm but not exceeding 3,000 nm.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A005</strong></td><td>“Lasers”, components and optical equipment, as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 6A205.<br>1. NOTES: Pulsed “lasers” include those that run in a continuous wave (CW) mode with pulses superimposed.<br>2. Pulse-excited “lasers” include those that run in a continuously excited mode with pulse excitation superimposed.<br>3. The control status of Raman “lasers” is determined by the parameters of the pumping source “lasers”. The pumping source “lasers” can be any of the “lasers” described below.<br>a. Gas “lasers”, as follows:<br>1. Excimer “lasers” having any of the following:<br>a. An output wavelength not exceeding 150 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 50 mJ per pulse; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>b. An output wavelength exceeding 150 nm but not exceeding 190 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 1.5 J per pulse; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 120 W;<br>c. An output wavelength exceeding 190 nm but not exceeding 360 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 10 J per pulse; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 500 W; or<br>d. An output wavelength exceeding 360 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 1.5 J per pulse; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 30 W;<br>2. Metal vapour “lasers”, as follows:<br>a. Copper (Cu) “lasers” with an average or CW output power exceeding 20 W;<br>b. Gold (Au) “lasers” with an average or CW output power exceeding 5 W;<br>c. Sodium (Na) “lasers” with an output power exceeding 5 W;<br>d. Barium (Ba) “lasers” with an average or CW output power exceeding 2 W;<br>3. Carbon monoxide (CO) “lasers” having either:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 2 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 5 kW; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 5 kW;<br>4. Carbon dioxide (CO<sub>2</sub>) “lasers” having any of the following:<br>a. A CW output power exceeding 10 kW;<br>b. A pulsed output with a “pulse duration” exceeding 10 microseconds and:<br>1. An average output power exceeding 10 kW; or<br>2. A pulsed “peak power” exceeding 100 kW; or<br>c. A pulsed output with a “pulse duration” equal to or less than 10 microseconds and:<br>1. A pulse energy exceeding 5 J per pulse and “peak power” exceeding 2.5 kW; or<br>2. An average output power exceeding 2.5 kW;<br>5. “Chemical lasers”, as follows:<br>a. Hydrogen Fluoride (HF) “lasers”;<br>b. Deuterium Fluoride (DF) “lasers”;<br>c. “Transfer lasers”:<br>1. Oxygen Iodine (O<sub>2</sub>-I) “lasers”;<br>2. Deuterium Fluoride-Carbon dioxide (DF-CO<sub>2</sub>) “lasers”;<br>6. Gas discharge and ion “lasers”, i.e., krypton ion or argon ion “lasers”, as follows:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 1.5 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 50 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 50 W; or<br>7. Other gas “lasers”, except nitrogen “lasers”, having any of the following:<br>a. An output wavelength not exceeding 150 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 50 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>b. An output wavelength exceeding 150 nm but not exceeding 800 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 1.5 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 30 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 30 W;<br>c. An output wavelength exceeding 800 nm but not exceeding 1,400 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 0.25 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 10 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 10 W; or<br>d. An output wavelength exceeding 1400 nm and an average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>b. Semiconductor “lasers”, as follows:<br>Technical Note: Semiconductor “lasers” are commonly called “laser” diodes.<br>NOTES: The control status of semiconductor “lasers” specially designed for other equipment is determined by the control status of the other equipment.<br>1. Individual, single-transverse mode semiconductor “lasers” having:<br>a. An average output power exceeding 100 mW; or<br>b. A wavelength exceeding 1,050 nm;<br>2. Individual, multiple-transverse mode semiconductor “lasers”, or arrays of individual semiconductor “lasers”, having:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 500 microjoules per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 10 W;<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 10 W; or<br>c. A wavelength exceeding 1050 nm;<br>c. Solid state “lasers”, as follows:<br>1. “Tunable” “lasers” having any of the following:<br>NOTE: 6A005.c.1. includes titanium-sapphire (Ti: Al<sub>2</sub>O<sub>3</sub>), thulium-YAG (Tm: YAG), thulium-YSGG (Tm: YSGG), alexandrite (Cr: BeAl<sub>2</sub>O<sub>4</sub>) and colour centre “lasers”.<br>a. An output wavelength less than 600 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 50 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>b. An output wavelength of 600 nm or more but not exceeding 1,400 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 1 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 20 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 20 W; or<br>c. An output wavelength exceeding 1,400 nm and:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 50 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>2. Non-“tunable” “lasers”, as follows:<br>NOTE: 6A005.c.2. includes atomic transition solid state “lasers”.<br>a. Ruby “lasers” having an output energy exceeding 20 J per pulse;<br>b. Neodymium glass “lasers”, as follows:<br>1. “Q-switched lasers” having:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 20 J but not exceeding 50 J per pulse and an average output power exceeding 10 W; or<br>b. An output energy exceeding 50 J per pulse;<br>2.
