Non-“Q-switched lasers” having:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 50 J but not exceeding 100 J per pulse and an average output power exceeding 20 W; or<br>b. An output energy exceeding 100 J per pulse;<br>c. Neodymium-doped (other than glass) “lasers”, as follows, with an output wavelength exceeding 1,000 nm but not exceeding 1,100 nm:<br>NOTE: For Neodymium-doped (other than glass) “lasers” having an output wavelength not exceeding 1,000 nm or exceeding 1,100 nm, see 6A005.c.2.d.<br>1. Pulse excited, mode-locked, “Q-switched lasers” with a “pulse duration” of less than 1 ns and:<br>a. A “peak power” exceeding 5 GW;<br>b. An average output power exceeding 10 W; or<br>c. A pulsed energy exceeding 0.1 J;<br>2. Pulse-excited, “Q-switched lasers”, with a pulse duration equal to or more than 1 ns, and:<br>a. A single-transverse mode output with:<br>1. A “peak power” exceeding 100 MW;<br>2. An average output power exceeding 20 W; or<br>3. A pulsed energy exceeding 2 J; or<br>b. A multiple-transverse mode output with:<br>1. A “peak power” exceeding 200 MW;<br>2. An average output power exceeding 50 W; or<br>3. A pulsed energy exceeding 2 J;<br>3. Pulse-excited, non-“Q-switched lasers”, having:<br>a. A single-transverse mode output with:<br>1. “peak power” exceeding 500 kW; or<br>2. An average output power exceeding 150 W; or<br>b. A multiple-transverse mode output with:<br>1. A “peak power” exceeding 1 MW; or<br>2. An average power exceeding 500 W;<br>c. Continuously excited “lasers” having:<br>a. A single-transverse mode output with:<br>1. A “peak power” exceeding 500 kW; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 150 W; or<br>b. A multiple-transverse mode output with:<br>1. A “peak power” exceeding 1 MW; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 500 W;<br>4. Other non-“tunable” “lasers”, having any of the following:<br>1. A wavelength less than 150 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 50 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>2. A wavelength of 150 nm or more but not exceeding 800 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 1.5 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 30 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 30 W;<br>3. A wavelength exceeding 800 nm but not exceeding 1,400 nm, as follows:<br>a. “Q-switched lasers” with:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 0.5 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 50 W; or<br>2. An average output power exceeding:<br>10 W for single-mode “lasers”;<br>30 W for multimode “lasers”;<br>b. Non-“Q-switched lasers” with:<br>1. An output energy exceeding 2 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 50 W; or<br>2. An average or CW output power exceeding 50 W; or<br>4. A wavelength exceeding 1,400 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 100 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>3. Dye and other liquid “lasers”, having any of the following:<br>1. A wavelength less than 150 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 50 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>2. A wavelength of 150 nm or more but not exceeding 800 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 1.5 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 20 W;<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 20 W; or<br>c. A pulsed single longitudinal mode oscillator with an average output power exceeding 1 W and a repetition rate exceeding 1 kHz if the “pulse duration” is less than 100 ns;<br>3. A wavelength exceeding 800 nm but not exceeding 1,400 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 0.5 J per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 10 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 10 W; or<br>4. A wavelength exceeding 1,400 nm and:<br>a. An output energy exceeding 100 mJ per pulse and a pulsed “peak power” exceeding 1 W; or<br>b. An average or CW output power exceeding 1 W;<br>4. Free electron “lasers”;<br>5. Components, as follows:<br>1. Mirrors cooled either by active cooling or by heat pipe cooling;<br>Technical Note: Active cooling is a cooling technique for optical components using flowing fluids within the subsurface (nominally less than 1 mm below the optical surface) of the optical component to remove heat from the optic.<br>2. Optical mirrors or transmissive or partially transmissive optical or electro-optical components specially designed for use with specified “lasers”;<br>6. Optical equipment, as follows:<br>1. Dynamic wavefront (phase) measuring equipment capable of mapping at least 50 positions on a beam wavefront with:<br>a. Frame rates equal to or more than 100 Hz and phase discrimination of at least 5% of the beam’s wavelength; or<br>b. Frame rates equal to or more than 1000 Hz and phase discrimination of at least 20% of the beam’s wavelength;<br>2. “Laser” diagnostic equipment capable of measuring “Super-High Power Laser” (SHPL) system angular beam steering errors of equal to or less than 10 microradians;<br>3. Optical equipment, assemblies or components specially designed for a phased-array SHPL system for coherent beam combination to an accuracy of Lambda/10 at the designed wavelength, or 0.1 micrometre, whichever is the smaller;<br>4. Projection telescopes specially designed for use with SHPL systems.<br>NOTE: For shared aperture optical elements capable of operating in SHPL applications, see the Military Goods Controls.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A006</strong></td><td>“Magnetometers”, “magnetic gradiometers”, “intrinsic magnetic gradiometers” and compensation systems, and specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>NOTE: 6A006 does not control instruments specially designed for biomagnetic measurements for medical diagnostics, unless they incorporate unembedded sensors specified in 6A006.h.<br>a. “Magnetometers” using “superconductive”, optically pumped or nuclear precession (proton/Overhauser) technology having a “noise level” (sensitivity) lower (better) than 0.05 nT rms per square root Hz;<br>b. Induction coil “magnetometers” having a “noise level” (sensitivity) lower (better) than:<br>1. 0.05 nT rms per square root Hz at frequencies of less than 1 Hz;<br>2. 1 × 10<sup>−3</sup> nT rms per square root Hz at frequencies of 1 Hz or more but not exceeding 10 Hz; or<br>3. 1 × 10<sup>−4</sup> nT rms per square root Hz at frequencies exceeding 10 Hz;<br>c. Fibre optic “magnetometers” having a “noise level” (sensitivity) lower (better) than 1 nT rms per square root Hz;<br>d. “Magnetic gradiometers” using multiple “magnetometers” specified in 6A006.a., b. or c.;<br>e. Fibre optic “intrinsic magnetic gradiometers” having a magnetic gradient field “noise level” (sensitivity) lower (better) than 0.3 nT/m rms per square root Hz;<br>f. “Intrinsic magnetic gradiometers”, using technology other than fibre-optic technology, having a magnetic gradient field “noise level” (sensitivity) lower (better) than 0.015 nT/m rms per square root Hz;<br>g. Magnetic compensation systems for magnetic sensors designed for operation on mobile platforms;<br>h. “Superconductive” electromagnetic sensors, containing components manufactured from “superconductive” materials, as follows:<br>1. Designed for operation at temperatures below the “critical temperature” of at least one of their “superconductive” constituents (including Josephson effect devices or “superconductive” quantum interference devices (SQUIDS));<br>2. Designed for sensing electromagnetic field variations at frequencies of 1 kHz or less; and<br>3. Having any of the following characteristics:<br>a. Incorporating thin-film SQUIDS with a minimum feature size of less than 2 micrometres and with associated input and output coupling circuits;<br>b. Designed to operate with a magnetic field slew rate exceeding 1 × 10<sup>6</sup> magnetic flux quanta per second;<br>c. Designed to function without magnetic shielding in the earth’s ambient magnetic field; or<br>d. Having a temperature coefficient less (smaller) than 0.1 magnetic flux quantum/K.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A007</strong></td><td>Gravity meters (gravimeters) and gravity gradiometers, as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 6A107.<br>a. Gravity meters for ground use having a static accuracy of less (better) than 10 microgal;<br>NOTE: 6A007.a. does not control ground gravity meters of the quartz element (Worden) type.<br>b. Gravity meters for mobile platforms for ground, marine, submersible, space or airborne use having:<br>1. A static accuracy of less (better) than 0.7 milligal; and<br>2. An in-service (operational) accuracy of less (better) than 0.7 milligal with a time-to-steady-state registration of less than 2 minutes under any combination of attendant corrective compensations and motional influences;<br>c. Gravity gradiometers.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A008</strong></td><td>Radar systems, equipment and assemblies having any of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 6A108.<br>NOTE: 6A008 does not specify:<br>a. Secondary surveillance radar (SSR);<br>b. Car radar designed for collision prevention;<br>c. Displays or monitors used for air traffic control (ATC) having no more than 12 resolvable elements per mm;<br>d. Meteorological (weather) radar.<br>a. Operating at frequencies from 40 GHz to 230 GHz and having an average output power exceeding 100 mW;<br>b. Having a tunable bandwidth exceeding + 6.25% of the centre operating frequency;<br>Technical Note: The centre operating frequency equals one half of the sum of the highest plus the lowest specified operating frequencies.<br>c. Capable of operating simultaneously on more than two carrier frequencies;<br>d. Capable of operating in synthetic aperture (SAR), inverse synthetic aperture (ISAR) or sidelooking airborne (SLAR) radar mode;<br>e. Incorporating “electronically steerable phased array antennae”;<br>f. Capable of heightfinding non-cooperative targets;<br>NOTE: 6A008.f. does not specify precision approach radar equipment (PAR) conforming with ICAO standards.;<br>g. Designed specially for airborne (balloon or airframe mounted) operation and having Doppler signal processing for the detection of moving targets;<br>h. Employing processing of radar signals using:<br>1. “Radar spread spectrum” techniques; or<br>2. “Radar frequency agility” techniques;<br>i. Providing ground-based operation with a maximum “instrumented range” exceeding 185 km;<br>NOTE: 6A008.i. does not control:<br>a. Fishing ground surveillance radar;<br>b. Ground radar equipment specially designed for enroute air traffic control and “software” specially designed for the “use” thereof, provided:<br>1. It has a maximum “instrumented range” of 500 km or less;<br>2. It is configured so that radar target data can be transmitted only one way from the radar site to one or more civil ATC centres;<br>3. It contains no provisions for remote control of the radar scan rate from the enroute ATC centre; and<br>4. It is to be permanently installed.<br>N.B. The “use” “software” must be limited to “object code” and the minimum amount of “source code” necessary for installation, operation or maintenance.<br>j. “Laser” radar or Light Detection and Ranging (LIDAR) equipment, having either of the following:<br>1. “Space-qualified”; or<br>2. Employing coherent heterodyne or homodyne detection techniques and having an angular resolution of less (better) than 20 microradians;<br>NOTE: 6A008.j. does not specify LIDAR equipment specially designed for surveying or for meteorological observation.<br>k. Having signal processing sub-systems using “pulse compression” with:<br>1. A “pulse compression” ratio exceeding 150; or<br>2. A pulse width of less than 200 ns; or<br>l. Having data processing sub-systems with:<br>1. “Automatic target tracking” providing, at any antenna rotation, the predicted target position beyond the time of the next antenna beam passage;<br>NOTE: 6A008.l.1. does not specify conflict alert capability in ATC systems, or marine or harbour radar.<br>2. Calculation of target velocity from primary radar having non-periodic (variable) scanning rates;<br>3. Processing for automatic pattern recognition (feature extraction) and comparison with target characteristic data bases (waveforms or imagery) to identify or classify targets; or<br>4. Superposition and correlation, or fusion, of target data from two or more “geographically dispersed” and “interconnected radar sensors” to enhance and discriminate targets.<br>NOTE: 6A008.l.4. does not specify systems, equipment and assemblies used for marine traffic control.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A102</strong></td><td>Radiation hardened detectors, other than those specified in 6A002, for use in protecting against nuclear effects (e.g. electromagnetic pulse (EMP), X-rays, combined blast and thermal effects) and usable for “missiles”, designed or rated to withstand radiation levels which meet or exceed a total irradiation dose of 5 × 10<sup>5</sup> rads (Si).<br>Technical Note: In 6A102, a detector is defined as a mechanical, electrical, optical or chemical device that automatically identifies and records, or registers a stimulus such as an environmental change in pressure or temperature, an electrical or electromagnetic signal or radiation from a radioactive material.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A107</strong></td><td>Specially designed components for gravity meters and gravity gradiometers specified in 6A007.b. and c.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A108</strong></td><td>Radar systems and tracking systems, other than those specified in entry 6A008, as follows:<br>a. Radar and laser radar systems designed or modified for use in systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104;<br>b. Precision tracking systems, usable for “missiles”, as follows:<br>1. Tracking systems which use a code translator in conjunction with either surface or airborne references or navigation satellite systems to provide real-time measurements of in-flight position and velocity;<br>2. Range instrumentation radars including associated optical/infrared trackers with all of the following capabilities:<br>a. Angular resolution better than 3 milliradians (0.5 mils);<br>b. Range of 30 km or greater with a range resolution better than 10 m rms;<br>c. Velocity resolution better than 3 m/s.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A202</strong></td><td>Photomultiplier tubes with a photocathode area of greater than 20 cm<sup>2</sup> having an anode pulse rise time of less than 1 ns.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A203</strong></td><td>Cameras and components, other than those specified in 6A003, as follows:<br>a. Mechanical rotating mirror cameras and specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br>1. Mechanical framing cameras with recording rates greater than 225,000 frames per second;<br>2. Streak cameras with writing speeds greater than 0.5 mm per microsecond;<br>Technical Note: Components of such cameras include specially designed synchronizing electronics and specially designed rotor assemblies (consisting of turbines, mirrors and bearings).<br>b. Electronic streak and framing cameras and tubes, as follows:<br>1. Electronic streak cameras capable of 50 ns or less time resolution and streak tubes therefor;<br>2. Electronic (or electronically shuttered) framing cameras capable of 50 ns or less frame exposure time;<br>3. Framing tubes and solid-state imaging devices for use with cameras specified in 6A203.b.2., as follows:<br>a. Proximity focused image intensifier tubes having the photocathode deposited on a transparent conductive coating to decrease photocathode sheet resistance;<br>b. Gate silicon intensifier target (SIT) videcon tubes, where a fast system allows gating the photoelectrons from the photocathode before they impinge on the SIT plate;<br>c. Kerr or pockel cell electro-optical shuttering; or<br>d. Other framing tubes and solid-state imaging devices having a fast-image gating time of less than 50 ns specially designed for cameras specified in 6A203.b.2.<br>c. Radiation-hardened TV cameras specially designed or rated as radiation hardened to withstand greater than 5 × 10<sup>4</sup> grays (Si)(5 × 10<sup>6</sup> rad (Si)) without operational degradation and specially designed lenses used therein.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A205</strong></td><td>“Lasers”, other than those specified in 6A005, as follows:<br>a. Argon ion lasers with greater than 40 W average output power operating at wavelengths between 400 nm and 515 nm;<br>b. Tunable pulsed single-mode dye oscillators capable of an average power output of greater than 1 W, a repetition rate greater than 1 kHz, a pulse less than 100 ns, and a wavelength between 300 nm and 800 nm;<br>c. Tunable pulsed dye laser amplifiers and oscillators, with an average power output of greater than 30W, a repetition rate greater than 1 kHz, a pulse width less than 100 ns, and a wavelength between 300 nm and 800 nm;<br>except:<br>Single mode oscillators;<br>d. Pulsed carbon dioxide lasers with a repetition rate greater than 250 Hz, an average power output of greater than 500 W, and a pulse of less than 200 ns operating at wavelengths between 9000 nm and 11000 nm;<br>e. Para-hydrogen Raman shifters designed to operate at 16 micrometre output wavelength and at a repetition rate greater than 250 Hz.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A225</strong></td><td>Velocity interferometers for measuring velocities in excess of 1 km/s during time intervals of less than 10 microsecond (VISARs, Doppler laser interferometers (DLIs), etc.).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6A226</strong></td><td>Pressure sensors, as follows:<br>a. Manganin gauges for pressures greater than 100 kilobars; or<br>b. Quartz pressure transducers for pressures greater than 100 kilobars.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6B</strong></td><td>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6B004</strong></td><td>a. Equipment for measuring absolute reflectance to an accuracy of +0.1% of the reflectance value;<br>b. Equipment other than optical surface scattering measurement equipment, having an unobscured aperture of more than 10 cm, specially designed for the non-contact optical measurement of a non-planar optical surface figure (profile) to an “accuracy” of 2 nm or less (better) against the required profile.<br>NOTE: 6B004 does not control microscopes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6B005</strong></td><td>Specially designed or modified equipment, including tools, dies, fixtures or gauges, as follows, and other specially designed components and accessories therefor:<br>a. For the manufacture or inspection of:<br>1. Free electron “laser” magnet wigglers;<br>2. Free electron “laser” photo injectors;<br>b. For the adjustment, to required tolerances, of the longitudinal magnetic field of free electron “lasers”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6B007</strong></td><td>Equipment to produce, align and calibrate land-based gravity meters with a static accuracy of better than 0.1 milligal.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6B008</strong></td><td>Pulse radar cross-section measurement systems having transmit pulse widths of 100 ns or less and specially designed components therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6B108</strong></td><td>Systems specially designed for radar cross section measurement usable for “missiles” and their subsystems.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6C</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6C002</strong></td><td>Optical Sensors:<br>a. Elemental tellurium (Te) of purity levels equal to or more than 99.9995%;<br>b. Single crystals of cadmium telluride (CdTe), cadmium zinc telluride (CdZnTe) or mercury cadmium telluride (HgCdTe) of any purity level, including epitaxial wafers thereof;<br>c. “Optical fibre preforms” specially designed for the manufacture of high birefringence fibres specified in 6A002.d.3.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6C004</strong></td><td>Optics:<br>1. Zinc selenide (ZnSe) and zinc sulphide (ZnS) “substrate blanks” produced by the chemical vapour deposition process:<br>1. Larger than 100 cm<sup>3</sup> in volume; or<br>2. Larger than 80 mm in diameter with a thickness equal to or more than 20 mm;<br>2. Boules of the following electro-optic materials:<br>1. Potassium titanyl arsenate (KTA);<br>2. Silver gallium selenide (AgGaSe<sub>2</sub>); or<br>3. Thallium arsenic selenide (Tl<sub>3</sub>AsSe<sub>3</sub>, also known as TAS);<br>3. Non-linear optical materials having:<br>1. Third order susceptibility (chi 3) equal to or less than 1 W/m<sup>2</sup>; and<br>2. A response time of less than 1 ms;<br>4. “Substrate blanks” of silicon carbide or beryllium beryllium (Be/Be) deposited materials exceeding 300 mm in diameter or major axis length;<br>5. Low optical absorption materials, as follows:<br>1. Bulk fluoride compounds containing ingredients with a purity of 99.999% or better;<br>NOTE: 6C004.e.1. specifies fluorides of zirconium or aluminium and variants.<br>2. Bulk fluoride glass made from compounds specified in 6C004.e.1.;<br>6. Glass, including fused silica, phosphate glass, fluorophosphate glass, zirconium fluoride (ZrF<sub>4</sub>) and hafnium fluoride (HfF<sub>4</sub>) with:<br>1. A hydroxyl ion (OH-) concentration of less than 5 ppm;<br>2. Integrated metallic purity levels of less than 1 ppm; and<br>3. High homogeneity (index of refraction variance) less than 5 × 10<sup>−6</sup>;<br>7. Synthetically produced diamond material with an absorption of less than 10<sup>−5</sup> cm<sup>−1</sup> for wavelengths exceeding 200 nm but not exceeding 14,000 nm;<br>8. “Optical fibre preforms” made from bulk fluoride compounds containing ingredients with a purity of 99.999% or better, specially designed for the manufacture of “fluoride fibres” specified in 6A004.f.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6C005</strong></td><td>Synthetic crystalline “laser” host material in unfinished form, as follows:<br>a. Titanium doped sapphire;<br>b. Alexandrite.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6D</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6D001</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “development” or “production” of equipment specified in 6A004, 6A005, 6A008 or 6B008.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6D002</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “use” of equipment specified in 6A002.b., or 6A008 or 6B008.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6D003</strong></td><td>Other “software”, as follows:<br>a. 1. “Software” specially designed for acoustic beam forming for the “real time processing” of acoustic data for passive reception using towed hydrophone arrays;<br>2. “Source code” for the “real time processing” of acoustic data for passive reception using towed hydrophone arrays;<br>b. 1. “Software” specially designed for magnetic compensation systems for magnetic sensors designed to operate on mobile platforms;<br>2. “Software” specially designed for magnetic anomaly detection on mobile platforms;<br>c. “Software” specially designed to correct motional influences of gravity meters or gravity gradiometers;<br>d. 1. Air Traffic Control “software” application “programmes” hosted on general purpose computers located at Air Traffic Control centres and capable of any of the following:<br>a. Processing and displaying more than 150 simultaneous “system tracks”;<br>b. Accepting radar target data from more than four primary radars; or<br>c. Automatically handing over primary radar target data (if not correlated with secondary surveillance radar (SSR) data) from the host ATC centre to another ATC centre;<br>2. “Software” for the design or “production” of radomes which:<br>a. Are specially designed to protect the “electronically steerable phased array antennae” specified in 6A008.e.; and<br>b. Limit the average side-lobe level increase by less than 13 dB for frequencies equal to or higher than 2 GHz.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6D102</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “use” of goods specified in 6A108.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6D103</strong></td><td>“Software” which processes post-flight, recorded data, obtained from the systems specified in 6A108.b., enabling determination of vehicle position throughout its flight path.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6E</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6E001</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development” of equipment, materials or “software” specified in 6A., 6B., 6C. or 6D.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6E002</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “production” of equipment or materials specified in 6A., 6B. or 6C.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6E003</strong></td><td>Other “technology”, as follows:<br>a. 1. Optical surface coating and treatment “technology” required to achieve uniformity of 99.5% or better for optical coatings 500 mm or more in diameter or major axis length and with a total loss (absorption and scatter) of less than 5 × 10<sup>−3</sup>;<br>2. Optical fabrication technologies, as follows:<br>a. For serially producing optical components at a rate exceeding 10 m<sup>2</sup> of surface area per year on any single spindle and with:<br>1. An area exceeding 1 m<sup>2</sup>; and<br>2. A surface figure exceeding lambda/10 rms at the designed wavelength;<br>b. Single point diamond turning techniques producing surface finish accuracies of better than 10 nm rms on non-planar surfaces exceeding 0.5 m<sup>2</sup>;<br>(N.B.: See also 2E003.d.)<br>b. 1. “Technology” for optical filters with a bandwidth equal to or less than 10 nm, a field of view (FOV) exceeding 40° and a resolution exceeding 0.75 line pairs per milliradian;<br>2. “Technology” “required” for the “development”, “production” or “use” of specially designed diagnostic instruments or targets in test facilities for “Super High Power Lasers” (SHPL) testing or testing or evaluation of materials irradiated by SHPL beams;<br>c. “Technology” “required” for the “development” or “production” of fluxgate “magnetometers” or fluxgate “magnetometer” systems having a noise level:<br>1. Less than 0.05 nT rms per square root Hz at frequencies of less than 1 Hz; or<br>2. 1 × 10<sup>−3</sup> nT rms per square root Hz at frequencies of 1 Hz or more.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6E101</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “use” of equipment or “software” specified in 6A002, 6A007.b. and c., 6A008, 6A102, 6A107, 6A108, 6B108, 6D102 or 6D103.<br>NOTE: This item only specifies “technology” for equipment specified in 6A008 when it is designed for airborne applications and is usable in “missiles”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>6E201</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “use” of equipment specified in 6A003, 6A005.a.1.c., 6A005.a.2.a., 6A005.c.1.b., 6A005.c.2.c.2., 6A005.c.2.d.2.b., 6A202, 6A203, 6A205, 6A225 or 6A226.</td></tr>
<tr><th colspan="2">Category 7 NAVIGATION AND AVIONICS</th></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A001</strong></td><td>Accelerometers designed for use in inertial navigation or guidance systems and having any of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7A101.<br>a. A “bias” “stability” of less (better) than 130 micro g with respect to a fixed calibration value over a period of one year;<br>b. A “scale factor” “stability” of less (better) than 130 ppm with respect to a fixed calibration value over a period of one year;<br>c. Specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 100 g;</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A002</strong></td><td>Gyros having any of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7A102.<br>a. A “drift rate” “stability”, when measured in a 1 g environment over a period of three months and with respect to a fixed calibration value, of:<br>1. Less (better) than 0.1° per hour when specified to function at linear acceleration levels below 10 g; or<br>2. Less (better) than 0.5° per hour when specified to function at linear acceleration levels from 10 g to 100 g inclusive;<br>b. Specified to function at linear acceleration levels above 100 g.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A003</strong></td><td>Inertial navigation systems (gimballed and strapdown) and inertial equipment for attitude, guidance or control having any of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7A103.<br>a. For “aircraft”:<br>1. Navigation error (free inertial) of 0.8 nautical mile per hour (50% Circular Error Probable (CEP)) or less (better) subsequent to normal alignment;<br>2. Not certified for use on “civil aircraft” by “civil aviation authorities”; or<br>3. Specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 10 g;<br>b. For land or “spacecraft”:<br>1. Navigation error (free inertial) of 0.8 nautical mile per hour (50% CEP) or less (better) subsequent to normal alignment; or<br>2. Specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 10 g.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A004</strong></td><td>Gyro-astro compasses, and other devices which derive position or orientation by means of automatically tracking celestial bodies or satellites, with an azimuth accuracy of equal to or less (better) than 5 seconds of arc.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7A104.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A005</strong></td><td>Global Positioning Satellite (GPS) receiving equipment having either of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7A105.<br>a. Employing encryption/decryption; or<br>b. A null-steerable antenna;</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A006</strong></td><td>Airborne altimeters operating at frequencies other than 4.2 to 4.4 GHz inclusive, having either of the following characteristics:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7A106.<br>a. 7A106 Power Management; or<br>b. Using phase shift key modulation.<br>(For automatic pilots of underwater vehicles, see category 8, for radar, see Category 6.)</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A101</strong></td><td>Accelerometers, other than those specified in entry 7A001, with a threshold of 0.05 g or less, or a linearity error within 0.25% of full scale output, or both, which are designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types and specially designed components therefor.<br>NOTE: 7A101 does not specify accelerometers which are specially designed and developed as MWD (Measurement While Drilling) Sensors for use in downhole well service operations.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A102</strong></td><td>All types of gyros, other than those specified in 7A002, usable in “missiles”, with a rated “drift rate” “stability” of less than 0.5° (1 sigma or rms) per hour in a 1 g environment and specially designed components therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A103</strong></td><td>Instrumentation, navigation equipment and systems, other than those specified in 7A003, as follows; and specially designed components therefor:<br>a. Inertial or other equipment using accelerometers or gyros specified in 7A001, 7A002, 7A101 or 7A102 and systems incorporating such equipment;<br>b. Integrated flight instrument systems, which include gyrostabilisers or automatic pilots, designed or modified for use in systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A104</strong></td><td>Gyro-astro compasses and other devices, other than those specified in 7A004, which derive position or orientation by means of automatically tracking celestial bodies or satellites and specially designed components therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A105</strong></td><td>Global Positioning Systems (GPS) or similar satellite receivers, other than those specified in 7A005, capable of providing navigation information under the following operational conditions and designed or modified for use in systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104;<br>a. At speeds in excess of 515 m/s; and<br>b. At altitudes in excess of 18 km.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A106</strong></td><td>Altimeters, other than those specified in 7A006, of radar or laser radar type, designed or modified for use in systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A115</strong></td><td>Passive sensors for determining bearing to specific electromagnetic source (direction finding equipment) or terrain characteristics, designed or modified for use in systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104.<br>NOTE: This item includes sensors for the following equipment:<br>a. Terrain contour mapping equipment;<br>b. Imaging sensor equipment;<br>c. Interferometer equipment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A116</strong></td><td>Flight control systems, as follows; designed or modified for systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104:<br>a. Hydraulic, mechanical, electro-optical, electro-mechanical or fly by wire types;<br>b. Attitude control equipment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7A117</strong></td><td>“Guidance sets”, usable in “missiles”, capable of achieving system accuracy of 3.33% or less of the range (e.g., a “CEP” of 10 km or less at a range of 300 km).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7B</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7B001</strong></td><td>Test, calibration or alignment equipment specially designed for equipment specified in 7A. except: equipment for Maintenance Level I or Maintenance Level II.<br>Technical Notes:<br>1. Maintenance Level I<br>The failure of an inertial navigation unit is detected on the aircraft by indications from the control and display unit (CDU) or by the status message from the corresponding sub-system. By following the manufacturer’s manual, the cause of the failure may be localised at the level of the malfunctioning line replaceable unit (LRU). The operator then removes the LRU and replaces it with a spare.<br>2. Maintenance Level II<br>The defective LRU is sent to the maintenance workshop (the manufacturer’s or that of the operator responsible for level II maintenance). At the maintenance workshop, the malfunctioning LRU is tested by various appropriate means to verify and localise the defective shop replaceable assembly (SRA) module responsible for the failure. This SRA is removed and replaced by an operative spare. The defective SRA (or possibly the complete LRU) is then shipped to the manufacturer.<br>N.B.: Maintenance Level II does not include the removal of specified accelerometers or gyro sensors from the SRA.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7B002</strong></td><td>Equipment, as follows, specially designed to characterize mirrors for ring “laser” gyros:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 7B102.<br>a. Scatterometers having a measurement accuracy of 10 ppm or less (better);<br>b. Profilometers having a measurement accuracy of 0.5 nm (5 angstrom) or less (better).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7B003</strong></td><td>Equipment specially designed for the production of equipment specified in 7A., including:<br>a. Gyro tuning test stations;<br>b. Gyro dynamic balance stations;<br>c. Gyro run-in/motor test stations;<br>d. Gyro evacuation and fill stations;<br>e. Centrifuge Fixture for Gyro bearing;<br>f. Accelerometer axis align stations;</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7B102</strong></td><td>Reflectometers specially designed to characterise mirrors, for “laser” gyros, having a measurement accuracy of 50 ppm or less (better).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7B103</strong></td><td>Specially designed “production facilities” for equipment specified by 7A117.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7C</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>None</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D001</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed or modified for the “development” or “production” of equipment specified in 7A. or 7B.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D002</strong></td><td>“Source code” for the “use” of any inertial navigation equipment or Attitude Heading Reference Systems (AHRS) (except: gimballed AHRS) including inertial equipment not specified in 7A003 or 7A004.<br>Technical Note: AHRS generally differ from inertial navigation systems (INS) in that an AHRS provides attitude heading information and normally does not provide the acceleration, velocity and position information associated with an INS.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D003</strong></td><td>Other “software”, as follows:<br>a. “Software” specially designed or modified to improve the operational performance or reduce the navigational error of systems to the levels specified in 7A003 or 7A004;<br>b. “Source code” for hybrid integrated systems which improves the operational performance or reduces the navigational error of systems to the level specified in 7A003 by continuously combining inertial data with any of the following navigation data:<br>1. Doppler radar velocity;<br>2. Global Positioning Satellite (GPS) references; or<br>3. Terrain data base;<br>c. “Source code” for integrated avionics or mission systems which combine sensor data and employ knowledge-based expert systems;<br>d. “Source code” for the “development” of:<br>1. Digital flight management systems for flight path optimization;<br>2. Integrated propulsion and flight control systems;<br>3. Fly-by-wire or fly-by-light control systems;<br>4. Fault-tolerant or self-reconfiguring “active flight control systems”;<br>5. Airborne automatic direction finding equipment;<br>6. Air data systems based on surface static data;<br>7. Raster-type head-up displays or three dimensional displays.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D101</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for the “use” of equipment specified in 7A001 to 7A006, 7A101 to 7A106, 7A115, 7B002, 7B003, 7B102 or 7B103.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D102</strong></td><td>Integration “software” for the equipment specified in 7A003 or 7A103.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7D103</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed for modelling or simulation of the “guidance sets” specified in 7A117 or for their design integration with the systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104.<br>Note: “Software” specified in 7D103 remains controlled when combined with specially designed hardware specified in 4A102.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E001</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development” of equipment or “software” specified in 7A., 7B. or 7D.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E002</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “production” of equipment specified in 7A. or 7B.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E003</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the repair, refurbishing or overhaul of equipment specified in 7A001 to 7A004;<br>except:<br>For maintenance “technology” directly associated with calibration, removal or replacement of damaged or unserviceable LRUs and SRAs of a “civil aircraft” as described in Maintenance Level I or Maintenance Level II (see Technical Notes to 7B001).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E004</strong></td><td>Other “technology”, as follows:<br>a. “Technology” for the “development” or “production” of:<br>1. Airborne automatic direction finding equipment operating at frequencies exceeding 5 MHz;<br>2. Air data systems based on surface static data only, i.e., which dispense with conventional air data probes;<br>3. Raster-type head-up displays or three dimensional displays for “aircraft”;<br>4. Inertial navigation systems or gyro-astro compasses containing accelerometers or gyros specified in 7A001 or 7A002;<br>b. “Development” “technology”, as follows, for “active flight control systems” (including fly-by-wire or fly-by-light):<br>1. Configuration design for interconnecting multiple microelectronic processing elements (on-board computers) to achieve “real time processing” for control law implementation;<br>2. Control law compensation for sensor location or dynamic airframe loads, i.e., compensation for sensor vibration environment or for variation of sensor location from the centre of gravity;<br>3. Electronic management of data redundancy or systems redundancy for fault detection, fault tolerance, fault isolation or reconfiguration;<br>NOTE: 7E004.b.3. does not specify “technology” for the design of physical redundancy.<br>4. Flight controls which permit inflight reconfiguration of force and moment controls for real time autonomous air vehicle control;<br>5. Integration of digital flight control, navigation and propulsion control data into a digital flight management system for flight path optimization;<br>except:<br>“Development” “technology” for aircraft flight instrument systems integrated solely for VOR, DME, ILS or MLS navigation or approaches;<br>6. Full authority digital flight control or multi sensor mission management systems incorporating knowledge-based expert systems; (For “technology” for Full Authority Digital Engine Control (FADEC), see 9E003.a.10.)<br>c. “Technology” for the “development” of helicopter systems, as follows:<br>1. Multi-axis fly-by-wire or fly-by-light controllers which combine the functions of at least two of the following into one controlling element:<br>a. Collective controls;<br>b. Cyclic controls;<br>c. Yaw controls;<br>2. “Circulation-controlled anti-torque or circulation- controlled directional control systems”;<br>3. Rotor blades incorporating “variable geometry airfoils” for use in systems using individual blade control.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E101</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “use” of equipment specified in 7A001 to 7A006, 7A101 to 7A106, 7A115 to 7A117, 7B002, 7B003, 7B102, 7B103, 7D101 to 7D103.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E102</strong></td><td>“Technology” for protection of avionics and electrical subsystems against electromagnetic pulse (EMP) and electromagnetic interference (EMI) hazards, from external sources, as follows:<br>a. Design “technology” for shielding systems;<br>b. Design “technology” for the configuration of hardened electrical circuits and subsystems;<br>c. Design “technology” for the determination of hardening criteria of a. and b. above.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>7E104</strong></td><td>“Technology” for the integration of the flight control, guidance, and propulsion data into a flight management system for optimization of rocket system trajectory.</td></tr>
<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 8 MARINE</th></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8A001</strong></td><td>Submersible vehicles or surface vessels, as follows:<br>NOTE: For the control status of equipment for submersible vehicles, see:<br>Category 5 “Information Security” for encrypted communication equipment;<br>Category 6 for sensors;<br>Categories 7 and 8 for navigation equipment;<br>Category 8.A. for underwater equipment.<br>a. Manned, tethered submersible vehicles designed to operate at depths exceeding 1000 m;<br>b. Manned, untethered submersible vehicles:<br>1. Designed to “operate autonomously” and having a lifting capacity of:<br>a. 10% or more of their weight in air; and<br>b. 15 kN or more;<br>2. Designed to operate at depths exceeding 1000 m; or<br>3. a. Designed to carry a crew of 4 or more;<br>b. Designed to “operate autonomously” for 10 hours or more;<br>c. Having a “range” of 25 nautical miles or more; and<br>d. Having a length of 21 m or less;<br>c. Unmanned, tethered submersible vehicles designed to operate at depths exceeding 1,000 m:<br>1. Designed for self-propelled manoeuvre using propulsion motors or thrusters specified in 8A002.a.2.; or<br>2. Having a fibre optic data link;<br>d. Unmanned, untethered submersible vehicles:<br>1. Designed for deciding a course relative to any geographical reference without real-time human assistance;<br>2. Having an acoustic data or command link; or<br>3. Having a fibre optic data or command link exceeding 1,000 m;<br>e. Ocean salvage systems with a lifting capacity exceeding 5 MN for salvaging objects from depths exceeding 250 m and having either of the following:<br>1. Dynamic positioning systems capable of position keeping within 20 m of a given point provided by the navigation system; or<br>2. Seafloor navigation and navigation integration systems for depths exceeding 1,000 m with positioning accuracies to within 10 m of a predetermined point;<br>f. Surface-effect vehicles (fully skirted variety) with a maximum design speed, fully loaded, exceeding 30 knots in a significant wave height of 1.25 m (Sea State 3) or more, a cushion pressure exceeding 3,830 Pa, and a light-ship-to-full-load displacement ratio of less than 0.7;<br>g. Surface-effect vehicles (rigid sidewalls) with a maximum design speed, fully loaded, exceeding 40 knots in a significant wave height of 3.25 m (Sea State 5) or more;<br>h. Hydrofoil vessels with active systems for automatically controlling foil systems, with a maximum design speed, fully loaded, of 40 knots or more in a significant wave height of 3.25 m (Sea State 5) or more;<br>i. Small waterplane area vessels with:<br>1. A full load displacement exceeding 500 tonnes with a maximum design speed, fully loaded, exceeding 35 knots in a significant wave height of 3.25 m (Sea State 5) or more; or<br>2. A full load displacement exceeding 1500 tonnes with a maximum design speed, fully loaded, exceeding 25 knots in a significant wave height of 4 m (Sea State 6) or more;<br>Technical Note: A small waterplane area vessel is defined by the following formula: waterplane area at an operational design draft less than 2 × (displaced volume at the operational design draught)<sup>2</sup>/<sup>3</sup>.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8A002</strong></td><td>Systems or equipment, as follows:<br>NOTE: For underwater communications systems, see Category 5.—Telecommunications.<br>a. Systems or equipment, specially designed or modified for submersible vehicles, designed to operate at depths exceeding 1000 m, as follows:<br>1. Pressure housings or pressure hulls with a maximum inside chamber diameter exceeding 1.5 m;<br>2. Direct current propulsion motors or thrusters;<br>3. Umbilical cables, and connectors therefor, using optical fibre and having synthetic strength members;<br>b. Systems specially designed or modified for the automated control of the motion of equipment for submersible vehicles specified in 8A001 using navigation data and having closed loop servo-controls to:<br>1. Enable a vehicle to move within 10 m of a predetermined point in the water column;<br>2. Maintain the position of the vehicle within 10 m of a predetermined point in the water column; or<br>3. Maintain the position of the vehicle within 10 m while following a cable on or under the seabed;<br>c. Fibre optic hull penetrators or connectors;<br>d. Underwater vision systems, as follows:<br>1. a. Television systems (comprising camera, lights, monitoring and signal transmission equipment) having a limiting resolution when measured in air of more than 500 lines and specially designed or modified for remote operation with a submersible vehicle; or<br>b. Underwater television cameras having a limiting resolution when measured in air of more than 700 lines;<br>Technical Note: Limiting resolution in television is a measure of horizontal resolution usually expressed in terms of the maximum number of lines per picture height discriminated on a test chart, using IEEE Standard 208/1960 or any equivalent standard.<br>2. Systems, specially designed or modified for remote operation with an underwater vehicle, employing techniques to minimise the effects of back scatter, including range-gated illuminators or “laser” systems;<br>3. Low light level television cameras specially designed or modified for underwater use containing:<br>a. Image intensifier tubes specified in 6A002.a.2.a.; and<br>b. More than 150000 “active pixels” per solid state area array;<br>e. Photographic still cameras specially designed or modified for underwater use, having a film format of 35 mm or larger, and:<br>1. Annotating the film with data provided by a source external to the camera;<br>2. Having autofocussing or remote focussing specially designed for underwater use;<br>3. Having automatic back focal distance correction; or<br>4. Having automatic compensation control specially designed to permit an underwater camera housing to be usable at depths exceeding 1,000 m;<br>f. Electronic imaging systems, specially designed or modified for underwater use, capable of storing digitally more than 50 exposed images;<br>g. Light systems, as follows, specially designed or modified for underwater use:<br>1. Stroboscopic light systems capable of a light output energy of more than 300 J per flash;<br>2. Argon arc light systems specially designed for use below 1,000 m;<br>h. “Robots” specially designed for underwater use, controlled by using a dedicated stored programme computer:<br>1. Having systems that control the “robot” using information from sensors which measure force or torque applied to an external object, distance to an external object, or tactile sense between the “robot” and an external object; or<br>2. Capable of exerting a force of 250 N or more or a torque of 250 Nm or more and using titanium based alloys or “fibrous or filamentary” “composite” materials in their structural members;<br>i. Remotely controlled articulated manipulators specially designed or modified for use with submersible vehicles:<br>1. Having systems which control the manipulator using the information from sensors which measure the torque or force applied to an external object, or tactile sense between the manipulator and an external object; or<br>2. Controlled by proportional master-slave techniques or by using a dedicated stored programme computer, and having 5 degrees of freedom of movement or more;<br>NOTE: Only functions having proportional control using positional feedback or by using a dedicated stored programme computer are counted when determining the number of degrees of freedom of movement.<br>j. Air independent power systems, as follows, specially designed for underwater use:<br>1. Brayton, Stirling or Rankine cycle engine air independent power systems having any of the following:<br>a. Chemical scrubber or absorber systems specially designed to remove carbon dioxide, carbon monoxide and particulates from recirculated engine exhaust;<br>b. Systems specially designed to use a monoatomic gas;<br>c. Devices or enclosures specially designed for underwater noise reduction in frequencies below 10 kHz, or special mounting devices for shock mitigation; or<br>d. Systems specially designed:<br>1. To pressurise the products of reaction or for fuel reformation;<br>2. To store the products of the reaction; and<br>3. To discharge the products of the reaction against a pressure of 100 kPa or more;<br>2. Diesel cycle engine air independent systems, having all of the following:<br>a. Chemical scrubber or absorber systems specially designed to remove carbon dioxide, carbon monoxide and particulates from recirculated engine exhaust;<br>b. Systems specially designed to use a monoatomic gas;<br>c. Devices or enclosures specially designed for underwater noise reduction in frequencies below 10 kHz or special mounting devices for shock mitigation; and<br>d. Specially designed exhaust systems that do not exhaust continuously the products of combustion;<br>3. Fuel cell air independent power systems with an output exceeding 2 kW having either of the following:<br>a. Devices or enclosures specially designed for underwater noise reduction in frequencies below 10 kHz or special mounting devices for shock mitigation; or<br>b. Systems specially designed:<br>1. To pressurise the products of reaction or for fuel reformation;<br>2. To store the products of the reaction; and<br>3. To discharge the products of the reaction against a pressure of 100 kPa or more;<br>k. Skirts, seals and fingers, as follows:<br>1. Designed for cushion pressures of 3,830 Pa or more, operating in a significant wave height of 1.25 m (Sea State 3) or more and specially designed for surface effect vehicles (fully skirted variety) specified in 8A001.f.;<br>2. Designed for cushion pressures of 6,224 Pa or more, operating in a significant wave height of 3.25 m (Sea State 5) or more and specially designed for surface effect vehicles (rigid sidewalls) specified in 8A001.g.;<br>l. Lift fans rated at more than 400 kW specially designed for surface effect vehicles specified in 8A001.f. or 8A001.g.;<br>m. Fully submerged subcavitating or supercavitating hydrofoils specially designed for vessels specified in 8A001.h.;<br>n. Active systems specially designed or modified to control automatically the sea-induced motion of vehicles or vessels specified in 8A001.f., g., h. or i.;<br>o. 1. Water-screw propeller or power transmission systems, as follows, specially designed for surface effect vehicles (fully skirted or rigid sidewall variety), hydrofoils or small waterplane area vessels specified in 8A001.f., g., h. or i.:<br>a. Supercavitating, super-ventilated, partially-submerged or surface piercing propellers rated at more than 7.5 MW;<br>b. Contrarotating propeller systems rated at more than 15 MW;<br>c. Systems employing pre-swirl or post-swirl techniques for smoothing the flow into a propeller;<br>d. Light-weight, high capacity (K factor exceeding 300) reduction gearing;<br>e. Power transmission shaft systems, incorporating “composite” material components, capable of transmitting more than 1 MW;<br>2. Water-screw propeller, power generation or transmission systems for use on vessels, as follows:<br>a. Controllable-pitch propellers and hub assemblies rated at more than 30 MW;<br>b. Internally liquid-cooled electric propulsion engines with a power output exceeding 2.5 MW;<br>c. “Superconductive” propulsion engines, or permanent magnet electric propulsion engines, with a power output exceeding 0.1 MW;<br>d. Power transmission shaft systems, incorporating “composite” material components, capable of transmitting more than 2 MW;<br>e. Ventilated or base-ventilated propeller systems rated at more than 2.5 MW;<br>3. Noise reduction systems for use on vessels of 1,000 tonnes displacement or more, as follows:<br>a. Noise reduction systems that attenuate at frequencies below 500 Hz and consist of compound acoustic mounts for the acoustic isolation of diesel engines, diesel generator sets, gas turbines, gas turbine generator sets, propulsion motors or propulsion reduction gears, specially designed for sound or vibration isolation, having an intermediate mass exceeding 30% of the equipment to be mounted;<br>b. Active noise reduction or cancellation systems, or magnetic bearings, specially designed for power transmission systems, and incorporating electronic control systems capable of actively reducing equipment vibration by the generation of anti-noise or anti-vibration signals directly to the source;<br>p. Pumpjet propulsion systems with a power output exceeding 2.5 MW using divergent nozzle and flow conditioning vane techniques to improve propulsive efficiency or reduce propulsion-generated underwater-radiated noise.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8B</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8B001</strong></td><td>Water tunnels, having a background noise of less than 100 dB (reference 1 micropascal, 1 Hz) in the frequency range from 0 to 500 Hz, designed for measuring acoustic fields generated by a hydro-flow around propulsion system models.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8C</strong></td><td><strong>Materials</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8C001</strong></td><td>Syntactic foam for underwater use:<br>a. Designed for marine depths exceeding 1,000 m; and<br>b. With a density less than 561 kg/m<sup>3</sup>.<br>Technical Note: Syntactic foam consists of hollow spheres of plastic or glass embedded in a resin matrix.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8D</strong></td><td><strong>Software</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8D001</strong></td><td>“Software” specially designed or modified for the “development”, “production” or “use” of equipment or materials specified in 8A., 8B. or 8C.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8D002</strong></td><td>Specific “software” specially designed or modified for the “development”, “production”, repair, overhaul or refurbishing (re-machining) of propellers specially designed for underwater noise reduction.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8E</strong></td><td><strong>Technology</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8E001</strong></td><td>“Technology” according to the General Technology Note for the “development” or “production” of equipment or materials specified in 8A., 8B. or 8C.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>8E002</strong></td><td>Other “technology”, as follows:<br>a. “Technology” for the “development”, “production”, repair, overhaul or refurbishing (re-machining) of propellers specially designed for underwater noise reduction;<br>b. “Technology” for the overhaul or refurbishing of equipment specified in 8A001, or 8A002.b., j., o. or p.</td></tr>
<tr><th colspan="2">CATEGORY 9 PROPULSION SYSTEMS, SPACE VEHICLES, AND RELATED EQUIPMENT</th></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A</strong></td><td><strong>Equipment, Assemblies and Components</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A001</strong></td><td>Aero gas turbine engines incorporating any of the technologies specified in 9E003.a., as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A101.<br>a. Not certified for the specific “civil aircraft” for which they are intended;<br>b. Not certified for civil use by “civil aviation authorities”;<br>c. Designed to cruise at speeds exceeding Mach 1.2 for more than thirty minutes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A002</strong></td><td>Marine gas turbine engines with an ISO standard continuous power rating of 24,245 kW or more and a specific fuel consumption of less than 0.219 kg/kWh at any point in the power range from 35 to 100%, and specially designed assemblies and components therefor.<br>NOTE: The term ‘marine gas turbine engines’ includes those industrial, or aero-derivatives, gas turbine engines adapted for marine propulsion or shipboard power generation.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A003</strong></td><td>Specially designed assemblies and components, incorporating any of the technologies specified in 9E003.a., for the following gas turbine engine propulsion systems:<br>a. Specified in 9A001; or<br>b. Whose design or production origins are unknown to the manufacturer.<br>NOTE: 9A003 does not specify multiple domed combustors operating at average burner outlet temperatures equal to or less than 1813 K (1540°C).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A004</strong></td><td>Space launch vehicles or “spacecraft” (not including their payloads).<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A104.<br>(For the control status of goods contained in “spacecraft” payloads, see the appropriate Categories.)</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A005</strong></td><td>Liquid rocket propulsion systems containing any of the systems or components specified in 9A006.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A105 and 9A119.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A006</strong></td><td>Systems or components, as follows, specially designed for liquid rocket propulsion systems:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A106 and 9A108.<br>a. Cryogenic refrigerators, flightweight dewars, cryogenic heat pipes or cryogenic systems specially designed for use in space vehicles and capable of restricting cryogenic fluid losses to less than 30% per year;<br>b. Cryogenic containers or closed-cycle refrigeration systems capable of providing temperatures of 100 K (−173°C) or less for “aircraft” capable of sustained flight at speeds exceeding Mach 3, launch vehicles or “spacecraft”;<br>c. Slush hydrogen storage or transfer systems;<br>d. High pressure (exceeding 17.5 MPa) turbo pumps, pump components or their associated gas generator or expander cycle turbine drive systems;<br>e. High-pressure (exceeding 10.6 MPa) thrust chambers and nozzles therefor;<br>f. Propellant storage systems using the principle of capillary containment or positive expulsion (i.e., with flexible bladders).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A007</strong></td><td>Solid rocket propulsion systems with any of the following:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A119.<br>a. 1. Total impulse capacity exceeding 1.1 MNs; or<br>2. Specific impulse of 2.4 kNs/kg or more when the nozzle flow is expanded to ambient sea level conditions for an adjusted chamber pressure of 7 MPa;<br>b. 1. Stage mass fractions exceeding 88%; and<br>2. Propellant solid loadings exceeding 86%;<br>c. Any of the components specified in 9A008; or<br>d. Insulation and propellant bonding systems using direct-bonded motor designs to provide a strong mechanical bond or a barrier to chemical migration between the solid propellant and case insulation material.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A008</strong></td><td>Components, as follows, specially designed for solid rocket propulsion systems:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A108.<br>a. Insulation and propellant bonding systems using liners to provide a strong mechanical bond or a barrier to chemical migration between the solid propellant and case insulation material;<br>b. Filament-wound “composite” motor cases exceeding 0.61 m in diameter or having structural efficiency ratios (PV/W) exceeding 25 km;<br>Technical Note: The structural efficiency ratio (PV/W) is the burst pressure (P) multiplied by the vessel volume (V) divided by the total pressure vessel weight (W).<br>c. Nozzles with thrust levels exceeding 45 kN or nozzle throat erosion rates of less than 0.075 mm/s;<br>d. Movable nozzle or secondary fluid injection thrust vector control systems capable of:<br>1. Omni-axial movement exceeding + 5°;<br>2. Angular vector rotations of 20°/s or more; or<br>3. Angular vector accelerations of 40°/s<sup>2</sup> or more.<br>Technical Note: For the purposes of 9A007.d. and 9A008.a., a strong mechanical bond means bond strength equal to or more than propellant strength.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A009</strong></td><td>Hybrid rocket propulsion systems with:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A109 and 9A119.<br>a. Total impulse capacity exceeding 1.1 MNs; or<br>b. Thrust levels exceeding 220 kN in vacuum exit conditions.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A010</strong></td><td>Specially designed components or structures, for launch vehicles or launch vehicle propulsion systems, manufactured using metal “matrix” “composite”, organic “composite”, ceramic “matrix” or intermetallic reinforced materials specified in 1C007 or 1C010.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 1A002 and 9A110.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A011</strong></td><td>Ramjet, scramjet or combined cycle engines and specially designed components therefor.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A111 and 9A118.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A101</strong></td><td>Lightweight turbojet and turbofan engines (including turbocompound engines) usable in “missiles”, other than those specified in 9A001, as follows;<br>a. Engines having both of the following characteristics:<br>1. Maximum thrust value greater than 1,000 N (achieved un-installed) excluding civil certified engines with a maximum thrust value greater than 8,890 N (achieved un-installed), and<br>2. Specific fuel consumption of 0.13kg/N/hr or less (at sea level static and standard conditions); or<br>b. Engines designed or modified for use in “missiles”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A104</strong></td><td>Sounding rockets, capable of a range of at least 300 km.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A004.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A105</strong></td><td>Liquid propellant rocket engines, as follows:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A119.<br>a. Liquid propellant rocket engines usable in “missiles”, other than those specified in 9A005, having a total impulse capacity of 1.1 MNs or greater;<br>b. Liquid propellant rocket engines usable in missiles with a range of 300 km or greater, other than those specified in 9A005 or 9A105.a., having a total impulse capacity of 0.841 MNs or greater.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A106</strong></td><td>Systems or components, other than those specified in 9A006, usable in “missiles”, as follows, specially designed for liquid rocket propulsion systems:<br>a. Ablative liners for thrust or combustion chambers;<br>b. Rocket nozzles;<br>c. Thrust vector control sub-systems;<br>Technical Note: Examples of methods of achieving thrust vector control specified in 9A106.c. are:<br>1. Flexible nozzle;<br>2. Fluid or secondary gas injection;<br>3. Movable engine or nozzle;<br>4. Deflection of exhaust gas stream (jet vanes or probes); or<br>5. Thrust tabs.<br>d. Liquid and slurry propellant (including oxidisers) control systems, and specially designed components therefor, designed or modified to operate in vibration environments of more than 10 g rms between 20 Hz and 2,000 Hz.<br>NOTE: The only servo valves and pumps specified in this item, are the following:<br>a. Servo valves designed for flow rates of 24 litres per minute or greater, at an absolute pressure of 7 MPa or greater, that have an actuator response time of less than 100 ms;<br>b. Pumps, for liquid propellants, with shaft speeds equal to or greater than 8,000 rpm or with discharge pressures equal to or greater than 7 MPa.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A107</strong></td><td>Solid propellant rocket engines, usable in missiles with a range of 300 km or greater, other than those specified in 9A007, having total impulse capacity of 0.841 MNs or greater.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A119.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A108</strong></td><td>Components, other than those specified in 9A008, usable in “missiles”, as follows, specially designed for solid rocket propulsion systems:<br>a. Rocket motor cases, “interior lining” and “insulation” therefor;<br>b. Rocket nozzles;<br>c. Thrust vector control sub-systems.<br>Technical Note: Examples of methods of achieving thrust vector control specified in 9A108.c. are:<br>1. Flexible nozzle;<br>2. Fluid or secondary gas injection;<br>3. Movable engine or nozzle;<br>4. Deflection of exhaust gas stream (jet vanes or probes); or<br>5. Thrust tabs.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A109</strong></td><td>Hybrid rocket motors, usable in “missiles”, other than those specified in 9A009, and specially designed components therefor.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A119.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A110</strong></td><td>Composite structures, laminates and manufactures thereof, other than those specified in entry 9A010, specially designed for use in the systems specified in entries 9A004 or 9A104 or the subsystems specified in entries 9A005, 9A007, 9A105.a., 9A106 to 9A108, 9A116 or 9A119, and resin impregnated fibre prepregs and metal coated fibre preforms therefor, made either with organic matrix or metal matrix utilising fibrous or filamentary reinforcements having a specific tensile strength greater than 7.62 × 10<sup>4</sup> m and a specific modulus greater than 3.18 × 10<sup>6</sup> m.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 1A002.<br>NOTE: The only resin impregnated fibre prepregs specified in entry 9A110 are those using resins with a glass transition temperature (T<sub>g</sub>), after cure, exceeding 418 K (145°C) as determined by ASTM D4065 or equivalent.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A111</strong></td><td>Pulse jet engines, usable in “missiles”, and specially designed components therefor.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9A011 and 9A118.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A115</strong></td><td>Launch support equipment, designed or modified for systems specified in 9A004 or 9A104, as follows:<br>a. Apparatus and devices for handling, control, activation or launching;<br>b. Vehicles for transport, handling, control, activation or launching.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A116</strong></td><td>Reentry vehicles, usable in “missiles”, and equipment designed or modified therefor, as follows:<br>a. Reentry vehicles;<br>b. Heat shields and components therefor fabricated of ceramic or ablative materials;<br>c. Heat sinks and components therefor fabricated of light-weight, high heat capacity materials;<br>d. Electronic equipment specially designed for reentry vehicles.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A117</strong></td><td>Staging mechanisms, separation mechanisms, and interstages, usable in “missiles”.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A118</strong></td><td>Devices to regulate combustion usable in engines, which are usable in “missiles”, specified in 9A011 or 9A111.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9A119</strong></td><td>Individual rocket stages, usable in missiles with a range of 300 km or greater, other than those specified in 9A005, 9A007, 9A009, 9A105, 9A107 and 9A109.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B</strong></td><td><strong>Test, Inspection and Production Equipment</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B001</strong></td><td>Specially designed equipment, tooling or fixtures, as follows, for manufacturing or measuring gas turbine blades, vanes or tip shroud castings:<br>a. Automated equipment using non-mechanical methods for measuring airfoil wall thickness;<br>b. Tooling, fixtures or measuring equipment for the “laser”, water jet or ECM/EDM hole drilling processes specified in 9E003.c.;<br>c. Directional solidification or single crystal casting equipment;<br>d. Ceramic cores or shells;<br>e. Ceramic core manufacturing equipment or tools;<br>f. Ceramic core leaching equipment;<br>g. Ceramic shell wax pattern preparation equipment;<br>h. Ceramic shell burn out or firing equipment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B002</strong></td><td>On-line (real time) control systems, instrumentation (including sensors) or automated data acquisition and processing equipment, specially designed for the development of gas turbine engines, assemblies or components incorporating technologies specified in 9E003.a.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B003</strong></td><td>Equipment specially designed for the production or test of gas turbine brush seals designed to operate at tip speeds exceeding 335 m/s, and specially designed parts or accessories therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B004</strong></td><td>Tools, dies or fixtures for the solid state joining of gas turbine “superalloy” or titanium components.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B005</strong></td><td>On-line (real time) control systems, instrumentation (including sensors) or automated data acquisition and processing equipment, specially designed for use with the following wind tunnels or devices:<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9B105<br>a. Wind tunnels designed for speeds of Mach 1.2 or more;<br>except:<br>Those specially designed for educational purposes and having a test section size (measured laterally) of less than 250 mm;<br>Technical Note: ‘Test section size’ in 9B005.a. means the diameter of the circle, or the side of a square, or the longest side of a rectangle, at the largest test section location.<br>b. Devices for simulating flow-environments at speeds exceeding Mach 5, including hot-shot tunnels, plasma arc tunnels, shock tubes, shock tunnels, gas tunnels and light gas guns;<br>c. Wind tunnels or devices, other than two-dimensional sections, capable of simulating Reynolds number flows exceeding 25 × 10<sup>6</sup>.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B006</strong></td><td>Specially designed acoustic vibration test equipment capable of producing sound pressure levels of 160 dB or more (referenced to 20 micropascals) with a rated output of 4 kW or more at a test cell temperature exceeding 1273 K (1000°C), and specially designed transducers, strain gauges, accelerometers, thermocouples or quartz heaters therefor.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B007</strong></td><td>Equipment specially designed for inspecting the integrity of rocket motors using non-destructive test (NDT) techniques other than planar X-ray or basic physical or chemical analysis.<br>(For radiographic equipment, see 3A001.e 5.)</td></tr>
<tr><td>9B008</td><td>Transducers specially designed for the direct measurement of the wall skin friction of the test flow with a stagnation temperature exceeding 833 K (560°C).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B009</strong></td><td>Tooling specially designed for producing turbine engine powder metallurgy rotor components capable of operating at stress levels of 60%percnt; of ultimate tensile strength (UTS) or more and metal temperatures of 873 K (600°C) or more.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B105</strong></td><td>Wind tunnels for speeds of Mach 0.9 or more, usable for “missiles” and their subsystems.<br>N.B.: SEE ALSO 9B005</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B106</strong></td><td>Environmental chambers and anechoic chambers, as follows:<br>a. Environmental chambers capable of simulating the following flight conditions:<br>1. Vibration environments of 10 g RMS or greater between 20 Hz and 2,000 Hz and imparting forces of 5 kN or greater; and<br>2. Altitudes of 15,000 m or greater; or<br>3. Temperature of at least 223 K (−50°C) to 398 K (+ 125°C).<br>b. Anechoic chambers capable of simulating the following flight conditions:<br>1. Acoustic environments at an overall sound pressure level of 140 dB or greater (referenced to 20 microPa) or with a rated power output of 4 kW or greater; and<br>2. Altitudes of 15,000 m or greater; or<br>3. Temperature of at least 223 K (−50°C) to 398 K (+ 125°C).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B115</strong></td><td>Specially designed “production equipment” for the systems, sub-systems and components specified in 9A005 to 9A009, 9A011, 9A101, 9A105 to 9A109, 9A111, 9A116 to 9A119.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B116</strong></td><td>Specially designed “production facilities” for the systems, sub-systems, and components specified in 9A004 to 9A009, 9A011, 9A101, 9A104 to 9A109, 9A111, 9A116 to 9A119.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>9B117</strong></td><td>Test benches and test stands for solid or liquid propellant rockets or rocket motors, having either of the following characteristics:<br>a. The capacity to handle more than 90 kN of thrust; or<br>b. Capable of simultaneously measuring the three axial thrust components.</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td>None</td></tr>
